# Changelog

One row per version of `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. A structural change adds a file
under `docs/changes/`; after the merge `scripts/record_changes.py` bumps the `version:`
there and writes its row here, in the order main landed the files.

| ver | date | change |
|---|---|---|
| 0.1049.0 | 2026-11-14 | The review bot and the autofixer's low tier move from DeepSeek V4.1 Flash to Claude Haiku 5.5 (`openrouter/anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`) at `xhigh` thinking. Artificial Analysis's free tier scores it 42.5 on its engineering index at $0.20 per million blended tokens, against 34.4 to 37.6 at $0.525 for DeepSeek. OpenRouter lists `xhigh` as a supported effort for it, so the level is not clamped. The medium and high tiers are unchanged. Both workflows drop the `wafer/mxfp4` routing pin written to `~/.yi/config.json`. It was DeepSeek-specific (a host measured fast for that model) and Haiku is not served there, so a strict pin would refuse every round. Revert by restoring the pin and the two model ids if Haiku rounds run slow. |
| 0.1048.0 | 2026-11-14 | Discovery now reads the output limit a provider advertises. It accepts `max_tokens`, then `max_output_tokens`, then `limit.output`, and falls back to `limit.context` for the context window. Before, a relay that advertised only `max_output_tokens` and `limit` (as OpenRelay does) had every discovered model capped at the 16,384 default. (Refs #2201) |
| 0.1047.0 | 2026-11-14 | A fan-out whose children died on their first model call could leave the parent unable to spawn or clean up. A child forked with `rlm.tree()` carries the parent's uncommitted files, and neither `merge_worktree` nor `discard_worktree` would release it, so `delete_subagent` refused forever. Folding such a finished child with `rlm.tree().merge(h.tree)` without conflict now releases its worktree the way `rlm.discard_worktree` does, so it can be deleted. A tree the discard refuses, such as a plan-managed child's, is named in a `notes` row of the reply. `rlm.discard_worktree(name)` works on it while it holds only the files it was forked with, and `force=True` drops changes the child made on top. The refusal names calls that work. A child that failed before its first tool call no longer counts toward the eight-child cap. A refused spawn says how many children are held and in what state (`8 of 8 children held (2 failed, 5 finished, 1 running)`), and a duplicate name names the child that holds it and the call that frees it. `rlm.run` now refuses a model whose `http` or `https` `base_url` is a loopback port that refuses connections, before taking a slot or a worktree. Folding a finished child whose tree still has a live job gives that job up to 250 ms to record its exit, where `rlm.discard_worktree` waits up to 5 s. A tree whose jobs outlive that is left in place, and the merge reply names `rlm.discard_worktree(name, force=True)`. The memory-label eval test that feeds `evals/memory_inject.py` a judge reply with no `usage.cost` now captures the refusal and asserts its exact text, so a reply that cannot be counted against `--cap-usd` is still refused by name. The refusal's `FAIL label:` line no longer reaches a gate log, where the commit hook read it as a failing gate and refused this branch's commits. (Refs #2168, #2169, #2170) |
| 0.1046.0 | 2026-11-14 | A custom provider can now omit `models` or leave it empty. Yi fetches its OpenAI-compatible `/models` response when the provider is first selected or listed, caches the result for the process, and applies any advertised context, output, and image-input capabilities. Explicit model entries continue to work without network discovery. (D598; Closes #2201) |
| 0.1045.0 | 2026-11-14 | Reclaim no longer cuts old tool results when the rewrite it forces costs more than the cut saves on this route. A cut changes the middle of the prompt, so everything after it is written to the cache again. The rule counted that rewrite as ten cache reads whatever the route charged, but Sonnet 5.5 charges 25 (write $2.50, read $0.10 per million tokens), and it counted the savings over every request sent so far, so the longer a session ran the easier a cut was to approve. In one recorded session 34 cuts made 35 requests rewrite the whole conversation, up to 195k tokens each, and held the cache rate at 84.3% against 97.2% without them. The rewrite is now counted at the route's write price over its read price when the catalog prices both, and still at ten reads for a route that prices cache at zero, twenty on such a route's hour cache. A cut's savings are counted over at most forty requests. A lapsed cache still cuts at once. (D597; Closes #2182) |
| 0.1044.0 | 2026-11-13 | `just baseline '<nextest filterset>'` runs a test on the fork point in a throwaway git worktree (a cold build into a temp target dir, deleted after), then on HEAD, and prints both exits, so "does it fail without my change" no longer needs a stash. It never touches the working tree. `scripts/suite_home.sh`, which recipes source, no longer kills a shell that sources it for something that is not a recipe: its guard now runs before it sets any shell option, so it prints why on stderr and returns 1 and leaves the caller's options as they were. Before, sourcing it from the bash tool exited silently with `exit code: 1`, and once skipped a `git stash pop` that was meant to follow. (Closes #2184) |
| 0.1043.0 | 2026-11-13 | `just pr review` no longer refuses to run when the review model is from the authors' family. It used `YI_REVIEW_MODEL`, else the configured primary, and returned 1 with `the review model ... is from anthropic, the authors' family; set YI_REVIEW_MODEL to another` when that was an Anthropic model. A round now runs on whatever model that names. `docs/FORGE.md` and the `pr-review` skill no longer say the model must differ from the authors' family. (D596; Closes #2199) |
| 0.1042.0 | 2026-11-13 | `just push` on a Mac now runs the Linux clippy step with the repo's zig wrappers as the C compiler and archiver when `zig` is installed, so `ring`'s C build finds a sysroot. Before, the step failed with `fatal error: 'assert.h' file not found` unless four variables were exported by hand. Without zig, a failed step prints the install command. `just push` also runs the net-deletion check that `just pr open` ran, so a branch that deletes lines with no `cut:` line is refused before the multi-minute push lane, not after it. `just pr open --help` says `--body` takes a file path. (Closes #2186) |
| 0.1041.0 | 2026-11-13 | A review round with a medium finding that holds the draft is now `blocked`, not `clean`. #2165 merged with five open medium findings, one of them a broken sentence that went into the changelog: the round read `clean` because only a high finding blocked, so `just pr ready` and the merge queue, which refuse only a blocked round, let it through, and the fixer, which answers drafts, never saw the mediums. A medium holds a round through round 2, and from round 3 on only while it is carried from an earlier round, as the draft rule already said. A fresh medium from round 3 on stays advice. The fixer answers a blocked round that has only mediums, and an `/override` still clears the round. (Closes #2172) |
| 0.1040.0 | 2026-11-13 | Tool results are coloured wherever they appear. A read that starts in the middle of a file, the tail after a long result's `… N more lines` row, and every search hit are coloured by the file's language. A search groups its hits and context rows under the file's path and drops the anchor tag. Shell and notebook output shows the colours the program printed instead of `␛[31m`, and any other escape sequence is dropped. JSON, diffs and scripts with a shebang are typed by their content, and file paths, `path:line:col` included, take the theme's status-path colour. The parse restarts from the grammar's first state at every break in the shown text, so a row that opens inside a string or a comment colours wrong at most until the next break. Painted card that opens inside a string or a comment colours wrong at most until the next break. Every surface paints a body as it draws it, with no cached copy: a memo keyed on the card's own text cost more to rebuild and hand back than the paint it saved, and the console never reached it. (D595; #2163) |
| 0.1039.0 | 2026-11-13 | Web search no longer throws away an answer because one of its citations has no excerpt. OpenRouter documents `url_citation.content` as added only "if available", but the parser required it, so a citation without one failed the whole paid search with `malformed web search response: url_citation.content is missing`, and the answer, the citations that did parse and the cost went with it. A citation without `content` now carries an empty excerpt. It still needs a `url` and a `title`, an annotation of another type is still skipped, and a search whose annotations all fail to parse is still the protocol error it was. The failure was seen on 2026-11-12 in a live search through `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`. The raw response was not kept, so the one citation that lacked `content` was never inspected. Nine later searches with the same query all returned five complete citations from one upstream, so the failure is intermittent and the unseen response is the only evidence for the fix beyond the documented schema at https://openrouter.ai/docs/features/web-search. (Closes #2161) |
| 0.1038.0 | 2026-11-12 | The chat composer keeps its draft on screen and draws assistance over it: a curly underline in the error color under a misspelled prose word, a gray suffix after the cursor that Tab accepts, and lists above the box for slash commands, `$` skills, `@` files, the fetch schemes Yi already has, and emoji shortcodes. A list opens only on a typed edit, so a pasted draft or a recalled history entry opens none and the arrows keep walking history. Tab takes what is drawn; with nothing drawn it completes the token at the cursor, in place for one match and as a list for several. Enter on a `/` row runs that command. Esc closes a list and keeps the draft. A `:name:` typed closed in prose becomes its emoji; code, paths and `std::x::y` keep their colons. `ctrl+.` lists spelling guesses where the terminal can send it. On a Mac the spelling is Apple's own checker, which ships with the system, reached through `osascript` in the background so typing never waits on it; nothing is downloaded. Elsewhere there are no typo marks, and completion uses the words of the user's recent prompts, in solo and the console alike; a headless drive has neither. If the checker fails or answers late, spelling is off for the session and one notice row says so. Autocorrect defaults off, and one ctrl+z undoes any replacement whole. Based on Max Ludwick's GitHub jackmaxwil/yi#9 (forge draft #2139). (D592, D593, D594; Closes #2158) |
| 0.1037.0 | 2026-11-12 | A hard link outside a credential store no longer reads a key through a tool, and no longer reads one unasked through bash. `read`, `grep`, `edit` and every other tool that opens through the read gate refuse a file whose identity is a store file, judged on the descriptor they opened, so a link swapped in after the check is refused too; grep's refusal row counts those files apart and names a cut scan when there is one. Bash cannot be judged by its words, since the sandbox matches the name a process opens and `cd`, `xargs`, `git grep` or a script reach the link unnamed. So in auto mode, while a store file has a name outside the stores, every bash command asks, naming the file, and an approved call still runs inside the sandbox; a rule, a grant or yolo that allows a direct store read allows this too. Removing the outside name ends it. The searched tree is never walked; only the stores are, once per check, so `rg foo` in a large repo runs as before. If the stores hold more than 10,000 entries or one cannot be read, only a named path with two names asks, and the ask says why. The stores are read once per bash decision, about 3 ms here, and to the cap when they are too big; nothing is cached, so a link made mid-session is seen. Kernel Python and an uncontained run are not judged by this. Contributed by Max Ludwick (GitHub #6, forge draft #2136). (D591; Closes #2145, Refs #2156) |
| 0.1036.0 | 2026-11-12 | The solo TUI and the console name the terminal tab `Yi`, a state mark and the session label. A four-frame text pulse runs every 250 ms while the focused foreground run works, `!` means it needs you and `>` means ready; background jobs and child sessions do not keep the parent title pulsing once its run ends. The label is whatever the session is already called: the latest prompt in the solo TUI, the summarizer's title in the console once it has one. Session naming itself is unchanged. Seen red: `title::a_card_shaped_reply_is_not_reformatted_into_a_card` stored `🧪 FLAKY: Fix flaky park tests` against a6aee8a49, the last head that carried the card parser (7826dc8b0 removed it). It stores `<title emoji="🧪" code="FLAKY">Fix flaky park` and is green on the head it lands against; it guards against reintroducing the card parser. (D590; Closes #2046) |
| 0.1035.0 | 2026-11-12 | Yi's palette can now be a theme. `theme.dark` and `theme.light` in `~/.yi/config.json` name a theme for that ground: one of the 104 that ship in the binary (the 100 oh-my-pi themes, whose MIT notice is in NOTICE, and four new ones, chalk, ember, iris and tide) or `~/.yi/themes/<name>.json` in oh-my-pi's shape, which wins over a shipped theme of the same name. The theme replaces the colors Yi already paints: accent, text, muted, dim, error, warning and success, the user-message and selection grounds, the syntax roles, the added and removed diff lines (drawn on Yi's own tints, and back to the built-in colour wherever the theme's falls under 3:1 against the tint), and the markdown, status and thinking colors. Pane borders, title chips and scroll thumbs keep their session hue. A 256-color terminal gets the nearest cube color for each, a 16-color terminal keeps its own colors and says so once. In the console, Dark theme and Light theme in the settings pane open the list under the row; moving through it paints the console and every open chat, Enter saves, and Esc, another row, a moved focus or a closed pane put the palette back. The row for the other ground saves without painting. In the solo TUI, `/theme` does the same for this terminal's ground. A preview repaints the live rows only, so scrollback and a pane's scroll position stay where they were, and a save rewrites the transcript in the new colors the way a resize does. A theme file that cannot load keeps the built-in palette and names why: a name that is not a var, a chain through more than 16 vars, a file past 256 KB, or a path that is not a file. The shipped light-prism named a `muted` var it never defined, upstream too; its copy here defines it. Contributed by Max Ludwick (GitHub jackmaxwil/yi#8, forge draft #2138). (D589; Closes #2147) |
| 0.1034.0 | 2026-11-12 | A provider declared under `providers` in `~/.yi/config.json` can now speak OpenAI responses or Anthropic messages, not only chat completions. `"api": "anthropic-messages"` sends its requests to `<baseUrl>/v1/messages` with the key as `authorization: Bearer` and `anthropic-version`, and `"api": "openai-responses"` to `<baseUrl>/responses`; with no `api` it is the completions provider it was. A misspelled value refuses at load, naming the three that exist. Since each wire appends its own path, a Messages `baseUrl` stops before `/v1` and a Responses or completions one ends in it. `yi doctor`'s `providers` row now names each provider's api. This is what GitHub #6 needed from its OpenRelay provider: a local gateway serving both routes behind one bearer key. That change named the gateway in yi-types, shipped it an empty catalog and read its models from the refresh cache; a custom provider per route does the same with nothing reserved, since `api` is per provider: one entry with `"api": "openai-responses"` and one with `"api": "anthropic-messages"`, both naming the same key variable. Credit: Max Ludwick found the gap and wrote the first version. (D588; Closes #2144) |
| 0.1033.0 | 2026-11-12 | In the `/` command list the typed name lives in the bordered text box, with the matches above it. Tab writes the highlighted command there and leaves a trailing space for arguments. Enter runs the highlighted match, so `/mo` Enter runs `/model`. Esc and Ctrl-C close the list and empty the box, as before. A draft that is `/` plus a command prefix does the same on Tab: one match is filled in place, several open the list. The paste test that expected the typed text inside the list is rewritten for the new model: a paste into an open list now fills the box. A list Tab opened over a draft keeps that draft on Esc and Ctrl-C, and the list is capped to the screen height with a named `[…]` row so the box stays visible. (Refs #2142) |
| 0.1032.0 | 2026-11-12 | `/queue <message>` sends a follow-up. While a turn is running it waits and runs when that turn ends, instead of steering into the turn the way a typed line does. On an idle session it starts the turn. With no message it says what it needs and queues nothing. The verb is on the solo TUI list and on ACP `_yi/slash`. The solo TUI runs a slash verb on a plain thread, which had no runtime to spawn the turn on; the thread now enters the driver's runtime first. The same thread runs `/lanes`, `/land`, `/discard`, `/pr` and `/base`, so their runtime-gated steps (retiring a session's kernel, durable schedules, plan finish hooks) now run instead of being skipped on that thread. (Closes #2142) |
| 0.1031.0 | 2026-11-12 | A plan todo whose contract keeps failing now has to change course before it is claimed again. After two red verdicts on one attempt, the next `done` is refused before its check runs, and the refusal names the two ways out: `split` the todo into one to four children, which the parent then waits for, or `append` todos with `for_task` naming it. Either buys one claim. The third red blocks the todo on the user as before, and the user's unblock now buys one more claim instead of leaving the todo pending with every later `done` refused. A `split` or targeted `append` that would buy nothing is refused with the rung it met. `plan.done_refusal_cap` moves both gated rungs. The cap's block asks the user a question (try it once more, accept it, drop it). Their reply in the chat, picked up by the next `unblock`, buys one claim; nothing else does (where nobody can reply, as in `yi plan`, the refusal says to drop it or accept it from a session), and a `fail` then `retry` after the cap starts the new attempt at the count the old one reached. `split` parses on every surface (`{"op": "split", "label": L, "subtasks": [{"title", "acceptance", "contract"}]}`) but is not in the tool schema, so the request does not grow; the refusal carries the literal call, and the plan skill and the tool guide (`yi://tools/plan`) name it. A subtask key nothing reads is refused at the tool, so a misspelled `contract` never lands a child on the unverified path, while the journaled shape stays lenient. `readmit` and `split_parent` live in `Todo.extra` until `doc.rs` splits at a seam and types them (#2157). Imported from Max Ludwick's GitHub jackmaxwil/yi#6 (forge draft #2136). (D587; Closes #2146) |
| 0.1030.0 | 2026-11-12 | A request no longer carries a tool result that does not answer a call of the turn just before it. The replay drops a reply that failed or was stopped, and with it the tool calls it held, but it kept those calls' results, so Anthropic messages, OpenAI completions and OpenAI responses each received a result with no call and refused the request, on that turn and every turn after it. A result that arrived after a note had closed its call's turn was a second answer, beside the `No result provided` the replay already gave that call, and was refused the same way. Each such result is now a host notice in the user role, `[bash result omitted: no call before it in this request still awaits it; run the tool again for its output]`, sent after the results that do answer their call. The tool's own output is left out: replayed as user or assistant text, a fetched page would read as the person's or the model's own words. The tool name in the notice keeps only letters, digits and `_-.:`, at most 64 of them. The repair is in `transform_messages`, the one replay every wire's encoder runs, rather than in the Responses encoder alone, where GitHub #6 first fixed it. Credit: Max Ludwick found the refusal and wrote the first repair. (Closes #2143) |
| 0.1029.0 | 2026-11-12 | A checkout of the public mirror now passes the guardrail gates that need files the mirror does not carry. `bridge.py`, `scripts/mirror/scan.py`, the scan, change-history and record-history selfchecks each print a `skip` row naming the missing input there. A source checkout has `scripts/mirror`, so every one still runs and a missing fixture is still a failure. No skip fires when `CI` is set, so a forge job that lost `scripts/mirror` fails instead of passing. (D586; Closes #2141) |
| 0.1028.0 | 2026-11-12 | Sessions started in the directory the sidebar already groups them by now share one accent, in the sidebar, the palette tile, the pane hue and the kitty mark; each session keeps its own creature, seeded by its id as before, so two sessions in one folder differ by shape and agree by colour. A kitty image carries its hue in its pixels, so a hue that changes under a live row (its folder text changing on a poll, or another folder scrolling into view) now transmits the image again instead of leaving the old colour. (D585; Closes #2140) |
| 0.1027.0 | 2026-11-10 | The two write race tests are gone, and the four check-then-open race tests no longer time out on a Mac. Each write race test needed 5,000 swaps landing inside the window, and every write's `sync_all` is a full drive flush on macOS, about 5 ms. Alone on a quiet machine the link swap took 134 s and the parent swap 61 s, past nextest's 60 s kill, and beside them the two read race tests stalled on the same flushes and timed out too. Linux CI flushes cheaply, so only Macs saw it. The write tool's gate is now proven the way edit's and grep's are: one call per open, with the parent swapped to a link to the key store at each open, in 0.06 s. A write that opens by path instead of through the gate fails it. What it does not catch, and the race tests did, is a write that reopens by path after the gated open; the read race tests still cover that shape for read. For write, a name swapped after the open is refused by the staged replace's own identity check, which a unit test in crates/tools/src/tool.rs covers for a link swapped in before the rename. |
| 0.1026.0 | 2026-11-10 | The autofixer no longer refuses a push because it deletes lines the pull request added (Closes #2131). The fixer guard did that after the fixer had already been paid, and it could not tell a dedupe the review asked for from the cut of a README block the owner made on purpose. On PR 2130 it discarded a $3.38 fix this way. A deliberate cut or keep is protected by recording it in a change file, which `check_intent.py` holds on every branch whoever wrote it; the guard's two incident logs, #2062's README and #1829's upstream pin, were cases that record would have caught. The `autofix:hold` label stays as the owner's own stop. (D584) |
| 0.1025.0 | 2026-11-10 | `evals-weekly.yml` replaces `tbv4-weekly.yml`. Every Monday it builds today's main and last Monday's, clones the inner repos at their pins (`evals/drivers/inner_repos.sh`), and `evals/drivers/weekly.py` runs each leg once per arm and repetition before judging the pair with `levers.judge`. A leg whose predicted spend its cap cannot cover stops and is reported as capped. The first week of a month also runs the Terminal-Bench slice once on head, as a trend. `evals/report.py` prints one digest: the week's verdicts with their graded intervals and spend, the static prefix's bytes over its last budget moves, and the costliest sessions of any directories given. With `--html` it writes the same as one offline page with inline SVG charts. The job's posting step, which runs no measured code, puts the digest and the rows to paste on issue #100 and opens an issue naming the week's merges when a leg is rejected. (D583; Refs #2129) |
| 0.1024.0 | 2026-11-10 | Every benchmark suite a gate or a refine round may run is now one row in `evals/suites.json`: the inner loop's capability suite (revert level 3, split into development and validation seeds) and economy suite (the generated log, bugfix and reconcile families), Terminal-Bench v4 pinned by its digest, and SWE-Atlas QnA waiting for its digest. Each row carries its harness, per-trial price, task groups and the A/A calibration a verdict is judged against. `evals/suites.py --selfcheck` refuses a Harbor row not pinned by digest and a task in two groups, and a gate refuses a suite that is unknown, unpinned or uncalibrated, by name, before anything is built or paid for. The weekly legs now read their tasks and prices from the registry (the capability leg runs once per arm over its 30 seeds, $1.20 predicted under its $1.70 cap), and `evals/drivers/harbor.sh <suite>` runs any Harbor row through the Terminal-Bench sweep driver's caps and watcher. Adding a Harbor dataset is one row: pin its digest, list the tasks this host can run, run an A/A, record it. (D582; Refs #2129) |
| 0.1023.0 | 2026-11-10 | A message's tool calls now run from their own module, `crates/loop/src/batch.rs`, and stop at the first write or command that fails: an edit the tool refused, a write or command the permission wall or a `--scope` glob denied, a write that errored, or a command that exited non-zero. Every call after it in the same message is answered "not executed", naming the call that failed and saying to send it again once that one succeeds, so a test queued behind a refused edit never reports on code that did not change. A failed read, search or todo call stops nothing, and after an interrupt the unrun calls still read as aborted. The new error kind is `not_run`; ACP shows such a call as cancelled and the eval surface does not count it as a tool failure. `yi stats` prints actions per request: tool results plus the nested calls a kernel cell made, over the session's assistant requests. (D581; Refs #2129) |
| 0.1022.0 | 2026-11-10 | `just refine` replaces `just improve`, and `evals/improve/` moves to `evals/refine/`. `just refine propose <base> <binary>` still asks Yi, in a container that sees only development-task data, for up to three variants. `just refine round <base> <binary> --usd N` walks variants (proposed, given with `--variant`, or queued by `/learn` in `~/.yi/refine/queue.jsonl`) through a cascade: S0 refuses a path outside the surface, `crates/types/src` included, and a variant already rejected; a code variant builds in a worktree and must pass the gate before any paid measurement; then the economy suite screens it, the capability suite validates it on fresh seeds, and Terminal-Bench checks that it transfers. Every stage stores its verdict row, a stage the remaining budget cannot cover is not run, and only a variant better at every stage is kept, as a `refine/<hash>` branch for a pull request. `skills/yi/refine/SKILL.md` teaches the same hill climb on any deliverable: a score that is a command, a frozen grader, one plan todo per variant, keep the best by the number, and stop at the budget. (D580; Refs #2129) |
| 0.1021.0 | 2026-11-10 | `/learn [focus]` turns the session it runs in into records. The verb sends a prompt (in the TUI a steer while a turn runs; over ACP queued behind it) that reads the new `learn` skill and follows it: find the failures, re-reads and corrections in this session's own log, quote the line behind each finding, save what should outlive the session as memory notes, and append at most three refine variants to `~/.yi/refine/queue.jsonl`, which `just refine round` reads. An item whose line cannot be quoted is dropped and named; a full queue (64 rows) takes no more. The verb writes nothing itself, and the skill never edits a prompt, rule or skill: a variant lands only as a pull request the owner merges after a round judges it better. (Refs #2129) |
| 0.1020.0 | 2026-11-10 | The session-mining extractor counts a harness census per session: requests, actions (tool results plus nested kernel calls), resolved re-check turns (a command-only turn that re-runs a check right after a clean edit), stale runs (calls that ran after a failed write or command in their own message), writes after a failure, spill reads with and without the line selector, and the subjects each doctrine section speaks to. `--sessions` takes several directories, and the report prints the census and a doctrine-relevance table by task family, counts only. `levers.py judge` takes two trial files and prints one verdict with its unit, its development spend and the tasks it needs to break even; `--store` appends the verdict row the improve round's S0 reads, so a rejected variant is no longer re-run. The economy road now passes on cost, then on wall while cost holds, and a route that bills nothing is judged by its tokens. A proposer's spend is recorded in its `s0.json`. `axes.py` prints an `a/r` line for the ledger's new rightmost column. (D578, D579; Refs #2129) |
| 0.1019.0 | 2026-11-10 | A session holding a message whose content block or stop reason this build does not know now loads instead of being refused as an invalid file. Before, one `{"type":"audio"}` block or one `"stopReason":"pauseTurn"` inside a `message` entry failed the whole line, because only an unknown entry `type` decoded to `Other`; an older daemon reading a session a newer build wrote after a variant landed lost the session. `Content` and `StopReason` now carry an `Other` arm: a block keeps its object as read and a stop reason its string, and both re-emit as serde_json writes them, field order included, the same canonical-JSON limit an unknown entry has (`1e5` comes back `100000.0`). A known tag that does not decode (a `text` block with no text) is still refused, and the error names the missing or mistyped field as it did before: `Content` and `StopReason` read the tag first and send only a tag outside their own set to `Other`. A block of an unknown type reaches no provider: the shared history transform, and the Cursor prompt, put the notice row `[… 1 content block left out: its type is newer than this build, which cannot read it; the session file keeps it as written, and no call here returns it]` where it sat, so the model knows something was there and never reads the block's own `type`, which is file text; the row names no fetch, because the model is shown no entry id to put in one. The TUI, console and ACP transcripts and a compaction summary's input read the same row; an explicit whole-entry read, `history://<agent>/<entry-id>` or `user://<n>`, returns the entry as written, block and type included, as it does an unknown entry. Search, brief lines and the other text extractions skip such a block as they skip an image. An unknown stop reason reads as a turn that ended: the run ends as `stop`, ACP reports `end_turn`, and the history keeps the message. `StopReason` is no longer `Copy`. (D577; Closes #2112) |
| 0.1018.0 | 2026-11-10 | Reasoning streams again while it is live, and folds once it settles. Before, the default view either committed a whole thought into scrollback or, in normal mode, hid it, and a tool card always showed three result rows, so long reasoning flooded the screen and short results were cut for nothing. Now a live thought shows its newest rows in a window of about a quarter of the screen under `thinking · 12s · 3.4k chars`, which never shrinks while the thought is live. Once the window cuts, its head says `last 8 rows · ctrl+o`, ctrl+o being the key that shows every thought whole. When it settles it becomes one row, `thought · 24s · 8.3k chars · ctrl+o`. A settled card keeps about a sixth of the screen's rows, twice that for a failed call, and shows the whole result when that is only one row more; a cut card ends `… N more lines`, and a failed one keeps its last lines after that count, where the error is. Verbose mode keeps every thought whole. In the console a press on a folded thought opens it beside the chat, the way Inspect opens a file. (D576; Refs #2094, Refs #2095) |
| 0.1017.0 | 2026-11-10 | `read` on an image refused it and pointed at `attach_image`, so the model spent a kernel round trip on every picture and the user saw a failed read. An image the provider takes now comes back as the image itself, under one line naming its type, pixel size and byte count, and carries the same `attachmentMedia` row an ipython attachment does, so reclaim can cut it to a re-attach once it ages (Closes #2093). An image the provider would refuse keeps a refusal: one past a limit names `attach_image`, which resizes it, and a BMP names the re-encode, both as one cell with the file's path; a non-vision model is handled by the request-side downgrade that already turns tool images into a placeholder. |
| 0.1016.0 | 2026-11-10 | Six hot paths stop building data they throw away, with no change in what any of them sends, stores or draws. The request's stable key hashes a streamed serialization of schema, tools and system instead of a `Value` tree and a 52 KB string: 1,085 allocations per request become 8, and the key is the same bytes D311 recorded, pinned against the first formula on the life-sized request context. A tool looked up by name reads `AgentTool::name` instead of building every tool's schema, about 1,100 allocations per tool call. The daemon relays a worker's plain `session/update` as the line it read, routed by a borrowed view of four fields (`AcpRoute`), and parses the frame only for a state change, a rename, a request or a field its view cannot borrow: 169,403 instructions per streamed delta become 104,426 in a real `yi serve`. The TUI reducer skips the escape parse a seam does not need, refills its live buffers in place and scans the unsettled block without copying it: a text delta costs 15.9 allocations instead of 27.8. A cached pane frame clones no row below the window: 2,574 allocations become 407 scrolled 400 rows up and 12,368 become 379 at the top. The SSE decoder reads each line in place: 20.15 allocations and 4.18 reallocations per event become 16.15 and 0.18. The message_end rise the red team saw at pace 100 (+177 allocations) is work the reveal moved, not work added: on a clock that steps 2 ms per event message_end costs 92 allocations before and 90 after, at pace 0 and pace 100 alike. On the wall clock faster deltas leave more unrevealed text for message_end to commit, and the message still costs less: its 2,000 text deltas and its message_end take 15,329 allocations, against 29,692 before. (Closes #2111) |
| 0.1015.0 | 2026-11-10 | Every command starts about a third faster. `yi --version` retires 25.4M instructions instead of 38.3M and `yi ask` 100.9M instead of 154.8M (minimum of 30 interleaved dist runs each). Three cuts: ureq's `native-certs` feature is gone, so every HTTPS call except the cursor handshake and `yi update`'s keychain fallback (providers, OAuth logins, MCP HTTP servers, the classifier call, the uv download) trusts the bundled web roots only and CoreFoundation no longer loads at startup (an MCP server behind a private CA now fails with `UnknownIssuer`, and `SSL_CERT_FILE` is no longer read); `Spill::new` compiles its nine redact regexes only when a spill directory exists, which no captured git call has; and `host_program` stats a PATH entry before canonicalizing it. The dist binary grows 31,984 bytes because the web roots no longer dead-strip. The 6 MiB cap is gone (D553), so nothing gates that. (Closes #2107) |
| 0.1014.0 | 2026-11-09 | Two journeys that failed on every push to main now pass (Closes #2125). The reclaim journey for an aged image scripted each reply with a 1 ms timestamp to make the cache look expired. Since the loop stamps every reply with the real clock, that expiry never happened and no result was cut; the journey now runs its script, ages the stored session by six minutes and resumes it, which is how a real lapse arrives. The `long-todo-label` surface scenario listed its three items as bullet lines, which the spec gate counts as three requirements, so the faux model's one-line stop was refused and its missing second reply ended the run with exit 1; the items are now plain lines, word for word the same. No product code changed. The reclaim journey went red on main before the change with `no image reclaim placeholder`, and the scenario with `FAIL surface_dry: rollouts did not exit 0: ['long-todo-label']`. A third failure on main, the ACP evicted-letter journey, does not reproduce on this Mac or in a Linux root container and is left to its own issue. |
| 0.1013.0 | 2026-11-09 | Every reply Yi stores now carries the time it ended (Closes #2109). Reclaim, which cuts old tool results out of the model's view, decides from the replies whether the route reads its cache and whether that cache has lapsed. No reply ever carried a time, so it saw none of them. On a route priced at $0 that still reads its cache, such as a subscription lane, it cut every aged result at once and rewrote the cache each time. It also never noticed a lapsed cache. A reply now gets the time it ended from the runtime's clock before it joins the history and the session file. Pi's writer stamps every reply too, at the start of the request; Yi stamps the end, as its cache record does, so a lapse is never read early. The field, its shape and its place in the bytes are unchanged. This also changes priced routes. Before, every reply was 0, so the lapse test never ran on any route. Now a session whose newest reply is older than its cache's lifetime (five minutes, or an hour) cuts every aged result at once as `expired` on its first request, whatever the route's price. That covers a resumed session and one the user left idle. The cache is cold by then, so the cut costs no extra rewrite. Sessions written before this change keep their 0 times. Reclaim still reads a 0 as no evidence, so an old session behaves exactly as before until its first new reply, which carries a time. The reclaim tests now stamp replies with a real epoch time, as production does. A new test runs a $0 route whose replies read the cache through the real loop and store, and checks that nothing is cut and every stored reply has a time. Another resumes a priced session six minutes after its last reply and checks that the first request cuts as `expired`. Those two session tests are the whole proof of the stamp: each goes red when the loop stops writing `StreamFn::now_ms` into the reply. The reclaim unit tests re-baselined beside them hold on the code before this change, reclaim having changed only in a comment, and prove the lapse rules they always proved, not the stamp. (D575) |
| 0.1012.0 | 2026-11-09 | The twelve har* skills are current on Rust 1.99.1, recommend nothing this repo's dependency law forbids, and a hand-edit of the copy under `.ruler/skills` now fails the build. Ten claims in them were wrong or stale, each checked against a primary source read on 2026-11-08. Valgrind was listed as working here and supports no arm64 Darwin at all, so the deterministic instruction-count lane is a Linux job. A HashDoS quote was attributed to hashbrown and belongs to foldhash. The rustc-hash algorithm claim had no source. `cargo llvm-lines` had no install line and is three characters from `cargo-llvm-cov`. The lld default is `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, not everywhere. `cargo-witness` was cited as an established tool and 404s on docs.rs, so the artifact-to-source gate is now recorded as having no mature tool, with the hand-diff that stands in for one. The `rand` bound was two majors out of date. har-threat's `frame()` did not compile: both `take` calls failed with E0507, not the E0277 this file first recorded, and the first fix added the error enum the `?` sites needed while leaving the `take` calls untouched. The block now imports `std::io::Read`, which is the whole defect — with `BufRead` alone `take` resolves through the supertrait and moves the reader, and `by_ref()` does not rescue it. Hand-checked on the pinned 1.94.0 in a scratch crate, including a drain that resynchronizes and one that exhausts `MAX_DRAIN` without a newline. Nothing in this repo compiles the rust blocks under `.ruler/skills`, so that check is not repeatable here and the snippet can regress with every gate green; a gate that builds them is worth its own branch. Nineteen recommendations contradicted YI_DESIGN.md section 18.3 or deny.toml by name. Every crate recommendation is now alternative-first: the dependency-free route leads, the crate follows with its cost, and where no dependency-free route exists the skill says so. That changes real advice in five places — har-layout's observability section, which now opens with a dependency-free stderr logger and states that spans are the one thing with no hand-rolled equivalent; har-hot-path's hashing, which leads with `BuildHasherDefault`; `static_assertions`, now a const assert; `CachePadded`, now `#[repr(align(128))]`; and har-threat's `subtle` requirement, which gained a written-out constant-time compare and the statement that Rust guarantees no constant time at the language level. Four gaps are closed, each a kind of code written here: flat storage and generational indexes, with the stale-index failure under a zero panic budget; a SIMD crate table built on who picks the instruction set and when; build profiles per package; and har-verify's case for Kani proving a panic budget in a crate that forbids `unsafe`, which is the rung this codebase most needs and least reaches for. The new gate locks a sha256 per skill rather than diffing against the canonical source, because that repository is not checked out in CI. The copy stays a copy and not a symlink, because `just install-skills` is `cp -R`. |
| 0.1011.0 | 2026-11-09 | Grep, read, the read gate and fork copy less, and every result they return is byte for byte what it was. `split` takes the file it splits by value and hands a file with no CR and no BOM straight back, with no copy and no per-line terminator list, since restoring it writes `\n` everywhere, so grep copies none of the plain files it searches. `normalize_to_lf` and the binary check use the std scans. Read walks one forward iterator across its sorted windows instead of holding a `Vec` of every line, so a 16 MiB file of newlines no longer costs 600 MB, and two hundred ranges near the end of a large file cost one pass. The read gate normalizes its protected trees once per gate instead of once per link hop, and its walls and spared paths once per check instead of once per hop, and it does not canonicalize a read's path a second time; the identities of the workspace git dirs still come from the spelling the kernel resolves, so a cwd spelled `link/../proj` keeps its `.git` walled (D323). Fork builds its store from the mutations it just wrote instead of reading and parsing the file again. (Closes #2110) |
| 0.1010.0 | 2026-11-09 | A `wait_s` of `1e30` or any other huge number passed to `tree.check` waits the 590 second maximum instead of panicking inside the standard library, which under the dist profile's `panic = "abort"` killed the whole `yi acp` worker for that root. A negative or non-numeric `wait_s` still takes the 30 second default. (Closes #2101) |
| 0.1009.0 | 2026-11-09 | The dist build compiles `regex-automata` at opt-level 3, in the per-package form `yi-orb` already uses. Resuming a 147-entry session in the TUI retires 3,887.7M instructions at "z" and 2,297.1M at 3 (-40.9%, 12 interleaved runs), for 148,608 bytes of binary. `ask` retires 6.2% fewer (154.1M to 144.5M), a gain that comes from the redact regexes compiling eagerly at startup and is not specific to this change's target path. `fancy-regex` or `syntect` at 3 instead cost 33,024 bytes and gain -0.5% and +0.3%, so the regex engine they sit on is the lever. The size-ledger header now cites D553, the row that removed the binary size gate, in place of D552. (Closes #2105) |
| 0.1008.0 | 2026-11-09 | The doctrine no longer restates the bash sandbox paragraph. Every fact it held (what leaves the sandbox, the "always" rule, the writable roots, the proxy, no unix socket) was already in the bash description. The two sentences with no twin moved there: a test that binds a unix socket fails inside the sandbox, and the repository's CI is where the answer lives. The system prompt is 544 bytes shorter and the bash description 96 bytes longer. (Closes #2103) |
| 0.1007.0 | 2026-11-09 | Eight items with no reference anywhere in src, tests, docs or scripts are deleted, with the syntect adoption handoff prompt: `PermissionBroker::clear_holds`, `LEDGER_TOOL`, `GREEN_STOP`, `Op::is_state_change`, `value_url` (and its import), hashline `with_path`, `JournalError::TailNotClean` and `ReduceError::UnknownKind`. The last was dead because an unknown op kind already fails as `ReduceError::Args` when `op_of` cannot decode its arguments, not because op kinds are a closed enum; `reduce` matches the kind as a string. Net -28 src lines; no behavior changes. (Closes #2100) |
| 0.1006.0 | 2026-11-08 | The reply prints once. A model that wrote its reply as text and also sent it through `answer` showed it twice, and the first copy had never passed the stop checks: in the recorded session the checks refused a draft whose words already stood on screen. Prose streams and commits as it always did. The loop appends an accepted draft as a message inside the turn whose reply called `answer`, the only model message a turn opens twice, and it appends one after the last word too, when the call itself fails. When that message starts, the drafts it repeats are emptied in place, so no other cell moves: the prose beside an `answer` with a body, and a plain reply the stop check held. A summary alone with nothing held heads the prose beside it, so that prose stays as the body. A refused draft stays until an appended answer replaces it, and a message cut off before its answer ran is never a draft. A stream with no turn events marks nothing appended. On resume there are no turn events, so the appended answer is the saved reply with no response id and no usage, the shape no provider writes. The solo chat rebuilds its scrollback from history once when it empties a cell, the same rebuild a resize runs, so rows past the reflow cap are not redrawn. The `answer` call draws no card, so neither "Answer delivered; the turn ends." nor a refusal addressed to the model reaches the screen. (Closes #2091, Closes #2092) |
| 0.1005.0 | 2026-11-08 | An image result was refused by every cut, because its placeholder had no way to say how to get the picture back, so a session that plotted for an hour resent every plot on every request. Reclaim now keeps the newest three image results whole and cuts an older one to `print(await attach_image("<path>"))`, the file named by the `attachmentMedia` row the ipython result records: the price and age rule is unchanged, and an image whose source file is gone, or no longer has the size and modification time the kernel stat before reading it, stays whole rather than being replaced by a command that restores a different picture (Closes #1551). The kernel records the absolute path it opened, so the host checks the file the kernel read whatever either process's cwd; the check is a stat, never a read, on the request path. An image result stored before this change has no recorded size or time, so it stays whole; the images a resumed session attaches from then on are reclaimed. A build without this change renders a stored image cut as the plain text placeholder: the picture is dropped from its view, and the history read it names returns the result's text, which names the file to attach again. |
| 0.1004.0 | 2026-11-08 | With `memory.importClaude` on, which is the default, every session start imports two directories before it reconciles the stores: the working directory's into the repository store, `~/.claude/memory` into the global one. Each note keeps its own file and gains `source: claude` as its first frontmatter key, so an imported note is told from a note Yi wrote; the index lines carry the Claude `MEMORY.md` wording where the source index had a line, and the store's own hook otherwise. A note that never closed its frontmatter imports too and is counted `unparsed` with the rest of the hand-written notes, and one that names an unreadable file no longer stops the batch: the count comes back as `unreadable` and the others land. An automatic start does not resurrect a note whose Yi store journal ends in `forget`; the explicit `yi memory import-claude` adopts it again. Source notes are listed and read once, so an unreadable one is counted instead of vanishing, and Claude index lines meet the same secret and loop refusal as note bodies. The session start reuses the index and notes the import reconciled, and the first prompt's block marks the stale ones. The import is idempotent by content, not by timestamp: each note file is compared byte for byte with the tagged source text, so a second start imports nothing, a changed source note updates its Yi memory, and a note deleted at the source is kept and ends its index line `⚠ stale`. `yi memory import-claude` runs the same import by hand whether the switch is on or not, `yi doctor`'s `claude-memory` row counts found, imported and stale without writing, and an import that leaves a store past the block's load cap appends the load row that names the cap and the call for the rest. `~/.claude` is read only, never written, and no note text reaches a log, a summary or a reply: counts and paths only. `yi memory import <dir>` keeps its own job and stays untagged, for any directory of notes; the import a session start runs is this one. (D574; Closes #1859) |
| 0.1003.0 | 2026-11-08 | The model's map of the kernel and its refusals both named things that do not exist. The surface note and the session state line called `help(rlm)` exact, which renders 34,337 bytes of the whole package; both now point at `help(rlm.<name>)`, one row's signature and docstring. The typing alias `rlm.HarnessScope` the signature table listed as a function left with the harness store (#1865), so the table needs no filter of its own. Four `SKILL.md` files under `python/skills` (`compact`, `goal`, `memory`, `attach-image`, 5,444 bytes) unpack to `~/.yi/python/skills`, which no skill root scans, so no model ever read them and the archive's `compact` text had already gone stale; `memory` and `compact` are covered by the host's `memory.*` handlers and the doctrine prompt, while `goal` and `attach-image` keep no copy, which loses nothing a model ever read; a test now refuses a shipped `SKILL.md` no root reaches. A refusal for a call nobody can approve ended "Run with --yolo, or add an allow rule for this call", a person's remedy in a model's context. The headless refusals name the three a model has: split the command, keep the writes inside the working tree, or ask the user. The approval classifier's breaker trip reached the journal only while the skill classifier delivered a notice on the same failure; `Approver::judge` now delivers one too, through one `breaker_notice` shared with the skill classifier. The model-facing `ask_user` answer for a headless run no longer tells it to ask the user again. The exact `permissions.allow` entry a headless denial prints for one call stays, since the operator of a headless `yi ask` pastes it to run that call; it now reads "The user can allow exactly this call with the permissions.allow entry …", and a pathless call says only the user in an interactive run can approve it. (Closes #1521) |
| 0.1002.0 | 2026-11-08 | A run now says why it ended. `AgentEnd` carries `reason`, a closed `RunEnd` vocabulary — `stop`, `error`, `aborted`, `length`, `guard`, `drained`, or an unknown tag re-emitted verbatim — filled at all five of the loop's exits, by the debug failed-turn end as `error`, and left absent by synthetic ends; a producer that sends no reason leaves the bytes of `agent_end` exactly as they were, so a Pi client and an older driver read the same line. `yi ask` decides its exit and its `no_answer` line from that reason instead of rebuilding it from the last reply's stop: a run whose `agent_end` says `error` closes as `error` and exits 1 even when the reply before it stopped cleanly. The stop-claim re-drives stop firing on text that only looks like a claim. `claims_impossible` matches each phrase at word boundaries, so "no validation errors" no longer reads as "no valid", and an answer-file re-drive weighs only a data path the prompt itself named: a `package.json` version or a yaml port written during the turn no longer earns one, while the answer file the prompt asked for still does. A `done` with no target keeps blocked rows open. The call reached every row only when nothing was runnable — a list whose rows all wait on someone else — and closed them with their asks; it now closes pending rows only, runs no check a kept row carries, and names each blocked row it kept in the result note. A goal releases its continuation after three runs that changed nothing. `GoalService` counts continuation runs that ended on an error, called no tool, or made only read-only calls, using the same predicate as the todo quiet-turn coupling. At three it stops continuing, marks the goal blocked and steers one notice naming the cap and the two ways back (`goal.update` to finish, `goal.objective` to restate). A single failed turn no longer blocks a goal on its own. A productive user-driven run resets the count, and a create, status update or objective edit starts the next objective with a fresh count. (Closes #1516) |
| 0.1001.0 | 2026-11-08 | `just pr retry N` now reads each failed gate job's log and reruns only runner-failed runs, at most twice per head. A compiler or test failure, an unreadable log or an unknown failure is never retried. The retry uses Forgejo's whole-run rerun because no single-job rerun is exposed; a run containing both failure classes is refused. `just pr merge` and `just land` use the retry automatically, while `just pr status` names it. Logs come through the existing `just ci-log` path in the infra repository-apply, and a signed PR comment is the per-head retry ledger. (Closes #2016) |
| 0.1000.0 | 2026-11-08 | The autofix pass no longer pushes a clean merge of main into a PR that is only behind, unless main changed a workflow's runner line the branch still carries, the stale-lane case where a branch's old copy holds retired runners. It still merges main in a scratch clone, with main's baseline driver, to find a real conflict, and a real conflict still reaches the fixer with its files named. A behind PR whose merge is clean now goes on to the same decision a current one gets, so a draft's carried findings reach the fixer instead of waiting behind a merge that changed nothing. The queue already builds each candidate on main, and the gate fixer already merges main when main explains a red lane, so nothing else was relying on the pushed merge. (Closes #2088) |
| 0.999.0 | 2026-11-08 | One open pull request owns a file. `scripts/owners.py` reads every open PR's changed files from its diff against main, and `just pr open` refuses a branch that changes a file another open PR changes, naming both numbers and the files, unless one is stacked on the other or the branch's change file says `shares: #N <why>`. land-fix and ns-lane live outside this repository and are to run `scripts/owners.py check --pr N` or `--paths …` the same way; until they do, `pr open` is the only caller. Change files, ledger lines, ADRs, baselines, AGENTS.md and Cargo.lock own nothing, since the recorder or a ratchet writes them for every branch. A change file's new `shares:` key is checked for its shape. (Closes #2086) |
| 0.998.0 | 2026-11-08 | A script no longer discards uncommitted work in a checkout it did not create. `scripts/checkout_guard.py check <dir> --by NAME` exits 1, naming the uncommitted paths, when `<dir>` holds work and NAME did not mark the checkout with `made`; a checkout handed to a person with `kept` loses its mark, and a directory that is no checkout of its own is a person's too. `just land` now refuses to fetch and merge `origin/main` into a checkout with uncommitted work. The land-fix and conflict-fix scripts outside the repo call `made` on the worktree they create, `kept` when they leave one for a person, and `check` before they remove one. (Closes #2080) |
| 0.997.0 | 2026-11-07 | The review bot reads recorded intent. Each lens is shown the `cut:` and `keep:` lines that touch the PR's files, from the PR's own change file and main's. A finding that would put back a cut line (it sits within three lines of where the PR deleted it) or delete a kept line (its quote is that line) is dropped before any refuter. The round lists each one with the change file that recorded it. On #2062, round 1's high asking to restore the owner's cut install block would have been dropped once the cut was recorded. (Closes #2084) |
| 0.996.0 | 2026-11-07 | A change file can now record what it removes or holds on purpose: `cut: <path> \| <the deleted line> \| <why>` and `keep: <path> \| <line> \| <why>`. `scripts/guardrails/check_intent.py` reads every landed change file's records at the branch's fork and refuses a branch, by any author, that puts a cut line back or deletes a kept one, naming the change file that recorded it; a branch overturns one with `revives:` or `drops: <change file> <path>` beside a `decision:`. It runs with the guardrails, so on every commit hook, push and queue candidate. `just pr open` refuses a branch that deletes five or more substantive lines from a file on net without a `cut:` line for it, or `cut: none <path>`. (Closes #2082) |
| 0.995.0 | 2026-11-07 | OpenRouter keeps a conversation on the host that last read its cache (Closes #2076). OpenRouter may answer a conversation on a different host between two requests, and the entry the old host wrote cannot be read there. The ledger already names that miss (`upstream switch`). Two of them inside thirty minutes now pin the host that last read the entry, and the next request on that route sends it first in `provider.order`, spelled as its OpenRouter slug, with fallbacks still allowed. The pin lasts an hour and is announced as a cache notice when it takes hold. The pin is scoped to the route that earned it. A request for another model never carries it, and a route change or a compaction ends it. It never replaces an `order` the routing config sets, because that order is the owner's. A host name from a reply that does not spell a plain slug is dropped rather than sent. PR #1829 first built this pin, and an automated fixer commit removed it before the PR merged. This change puts it back and answers the review findings that stood against it: the pin is scoped to its route, a configured order is never replaced, the provider object is built in `routing_params` alone, and a journey test covers the path from the ledger to the wire. (D573) |
| 0.994.0 | 2026-11-07 | A fixer's push is refused when it undoes what the pull request's own commits did on purpose. `scripts/fixer_guard.py` reads the commits a push adds against the PR's earlier commits: re-adding a line an earlier commit deleted, or deleting five or more substantive lines one added, refuses the push; a review finding that asked for it is shown beside the hit and exempts nothing, labels the PR `autofix:hold` and comments each hit. The autofixer runs it before every push; the land-fix script calls `fixer_guard.py --pr N --from <head before the fix>` before its own. Its selfcheck replays the two incidents that made it, #2062's README and #1829's upstream pin. (Closes #2075) |
| 0.993.0 | 2026-11-07 | A todo row now carries a check. `start` takes `check=`, stores it once and never replaces it; `done` runs it in the session's working directory under the checker's cleared environment and closes the row only when it exits 0, so a red leaves the row running with the output in view and `drop` with a reason is the only way past it. `start` probes a host-run command before the row is written: every segment's first word must be a program on `PATH`, so a check built on a typo is refused while nothing it would have run exists on disk. A container command leaves that judgment to its image. `arms` reports that command to the same rules, wall and gate as any other call, so a denied command never runs once, at start or at done. `rm` and a `set` that omits, changes or blanks an open checked row are refused, naming the `drop` that keeps the red visible; a green or terminal row is released like any other. The list's fingerprint appends a red's digest, computed from the output it produced: an unchanged red still climbs the stop ladder, and a red whose output moved resets it. `done` runs only the open rows in its scope and builds their evidence from the commands it ran, and a `done` the list would refuse, such as a parent with open children, runs no check at all. A dropped red stays as history without appearing in later stop text, and `answer` never runs a row's check. A check runs with a ten-minute deadline of its own; the session's remaining deadline is not threaded to it yet, because the tool context carries none. (Closes #1543) |
| 0.992.0 | 2026-11-07 | The kernel venv is now two layers. The dependency venv (`~/.yi/kernel-venv-<hash>`) is named by the bootstrap schema, the ipykernel and dill pins, the default extras and the dependency arrays of the yi_runtime and skill pyprojects; it holds ipykernel, dill and the extras. The yi_runtime package and the Python skills install `--no-deps` into an overlay, `<venv>/overlays/rt-<identity>`, which reads the dependency venv's site-packages through a `.pth` line, and the kernel runs the overlay's python. A change to a `.py` file therefore installs one new overlay in about a second instead of deleting and rebuilding the whole venv, and it never deletes the environment a running session imports from. Old overlays and old dependency venvs are swept only once they are a day settled, hold no bootstrap lock and no running process names them. A `YI_KERNEL_PYTHON` keeps a ready stamp under `~/.yi`, so a second boot over an unchanged interpreter runs no probes. Naming and the sweep live in `crates/kernel/src/venv.rs`. The kernel's `rlm.harness` store is gone: `rlm.harness`, `rlm.get_harness_state` and the `HarnessEntry`, `HarnessScope`, `HarnessState` and `RefinementEvent` types are deleted, along with the host's `harness.save_global` request and the `RLM_GLOBAL_HARNESS_STATE_DIR` and `RLM_GLOBAL_HARNESS_HOST` kernel environment. The ready check now asserts `rlm` has no `harness`. ## Seen red - `test_a_value_reads_only_the_keys_it_received`: `AttributeError not raised` when `total_lines` silently resolved the `totalLines` key. - `test_search_rows_keep_the_signature_and_whole_description`: the row was `{'name': 'fetch', 'summary': 'Read a URL'}` instead of the signature and full description. - `test_a_result_by_child_id_digests_the_wait_by_session_name`: `RuntimeError: child sub-1 (sub-1) is no longer registered with the parent` with a wait reply keyed by session name `n`, the host's wait-map shape. - `test_a_result_by_child_id_is_not_told_a_siblings_state`: `RuntimeError: child sub-1 is stuck (repeat break)` when the one child in the wait reply was a sibling, against the single-candidate guess this change deletes. - `test_mcp_public_names_remain_importable`: `AttributeError: module 'rlm' has no attribute 'McpToolError'` against the public names shipped before this release. - `a_new_runtime_identity_leaves_the_running_one_and_the_dependencies_alone`: `left: 4`, `right: 12` for missing-source warnings from one dependency venv and two overlays, using the existing fake uv fixture. (D572; Closes #1546) |
| 0.991.0 | 2026-11-07 | `yi stats <id>` billed only the messages it could fold, so a family's child lanes and a session's side calls, which book their spend as `usage` records and never as entries, went missing from the row: `--since` reads each child's own file and is right, but the single-session view was short wherever a child or a title, select, compact or branch call had spent. The fold now reads the ledger's `billed_usage` rows too, the same predicate the store bills its own `SessionStats` with, so `yi stats` and `yi rpc`'s `get_session_stats` report one number; a `cause: "assistant"` row mirrors an entry and still bills nothing. `yi rpc` answered every command inside its `select!`, so while `compact` awaited a summary the event arm was parked: a turn streaming into the broadcast buffer over it lost frames the client was never told it lost (`event_gap`), and a state query sent meanwhile answered only once the command ahead of it had finished. Each command now answers from its own task against shared state, so the loop never stops reading events and a reply lands in the order its command finished. Three Pi compatibility stubs are honest with it: `isCompacting` reports a compact in flight instead of a constant false, `get_commands` lists the RPC commands this transport answers rather than slash verbs it does not route, and `get_state` no longer echoes `steeringMode`/`followUpMode` values nothing reads, their setters answering with the reason instead. (Closes #1542) |
| 0.990.0 | 2026-11-07 | The sidebar's project order lived in memory for the run, so it was gone at the next start. It is now a config key, `console.projectOrder`: a list of project directories in the order the sidebar shows them. The console writes it through `_yi/set_default` after a header drag or ⌥⇧↑↓ and reads it when it starts, the way `console.sidebar` is kept. The key is optional and additive, so no existing config or wire shape changes, and the strict parse still names a wrong type by its key. The settings pane lists it beside the sidebar state as a restart-only row. A directory that no longer has sessions stays in the list and is ignored. (Closes #2065) |
| 0.989.0 | 2026-11-07 | The Files tree was a modal popup over the panes, and opening a file replaced the focused pane, so a lone chat was swapped for the file and filled the screen. It is now a VS Code style explorer on the right edge, folders before files with indent guides and refresh and collapse-all buttons in its title row, that stays up while a file opens in a panel beside the chat. A click on a folder row folds it, a click on a file row opens it, the wheel scrolls, and the row under the pointer lights up. A press on a pane hands the keys back, and ✕ or Esc closes the tree. A second file reuses the editor panel when its edits are saved and splits a fresh one when they are not, so a file never replaces unsaved work. The cap notice wraps inside the narrow sidebar instead of clipping. An empty chat and an empty notebook were a blank rectangle. Each now draws a centred start screen: the keys that start work, as click targets, and in a chat the five newest named sessions with their age. It disappears with the first row of transcript, and a replayed session never shows it. Panels move by zone. ⌘⌥⇧ with an arrow takes a half of the screen, ⌘⌥ with d, f or g a third, and a drag from a pane title, a session row or the new-session control lights the zone under the pointer and drops a panel there. Ghostty binds ⌘⌥ with an arrow to `goto_split` and ⌘D, ⌘F and ⌘G to its own split and search, so the arrows add ⇧ and the letters add ⌥ and none of them reach a chord Ghostty keeps. (D571; Closes #2063) |
| 0.988.0 | 2026-11-07 | The exec pool test counts distinct probe scripts, so a pipeline fork no longer reads as a fifth probe. (Closes #2071) |
| 0.987.0 | 2026-11-07 | The status row now says what each part is. Before, a console chat showed a bare `yi` and no session id at all, and a lane's branch was cut from the left down to the end of its long id, so nobody could tell which session, worktree or branch they were looking at. Now the row reads `worktree yi › branch ⎇ yi/01a1…c1059` on the left and `session 01 1b9c1059` on the right. A plain checkout shows `dir <path>` in the same place. The console's chat shows the id too, which it used to hide because the pane title carries the session's name. A narrow window drops the meters first, then the branch, and last the place itself, so each label goes only with the value it names. (D570; Closes #2058) |
| 0.986.0 | 2026-11-07 | The autofixer now merges the base into a draft PR branch after ten quiet minutes, not two hours, when the base changed a workflow's `runs-on` line since the branch left it and the branch's own copy of that workflow still carries the old line (D541). On 2026-10-12 `review.yml` moved to the `llm` runner, ten branches that had not merged main kept running their own copy on `ubuntu-latest`, and those runs held five of the six general runners for nearly an hour. A ready PR, a workflow the base added or deleted, and a branch that already moved the line are left alone, and `autofix:hold` still keeps the fixer out. (D569; Closes #1334) |
| 0.985.0 | 2026-11-07 | The `pr review` command starts a fresh round when the latest round did not read the current head. (D568; Closes #2069) |
| 0.984.0 | 2026-11-07 | `yi setup` is two numbered menus in plain words. The first asks which AI Yi should use: a flagship for Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter and Google, plus a model for any other provider a stored login already unlocks, the ones with a credential first and the first of them the Enter answer. A config that already names a model is offered "Keep" instead. Picking a provider with no key asks for it right there, with the page that makes one, and stores it as `yi login` does. The second asks how much Yi should ask before it acts and defaults to the mode already saved. A number or one word that names a single row both answer; a mistyped answer is asked again and is never read as a skip. Finishing with nothing chosen writes `{}`, as declining does, so the first-launch offer does not return on every launch. The classifier question is gone: `models.classifier` stays a config key, and `yi doctor` says so. `yi login anthropic` now takes a pasted key like openai, openrouter and google. It refused with "no login profile for anthropic" while the missing-key message told people to run it. A login whose models arrive only with the account's list (kimi, cursor) appears in the menu once that list has been fetched. (D567; Closes #2054) |
| 0.983.0 | 2026-11-07 | Moves with no behaviour change, so the files past their line cap have room for the tree work: `BranchName`, `Sha` and `Head` to `lane/names.rs`, a kernel child's hand-back to `lane/handback.rs`, the spawn-kwarg types to `subagent/build.rs`, `job_profile` to `kernel_state.rs`, and check capture to `goal/check.rs`. A container sweep that exits nonzero now reports its exit code instead of a sweep timeout; only a sweep that hits `SWEEP_BOUND` reports the bound. The outbox tests use the shared scratch cleanup. (Closes #1935) |
| 0.982.0 | 2026-11-07 | A child session can now find skills. Narrow workers, readers that can read, and full children are told where the skill list is, `read("yi://skills")`, which now serves every listed skill with its whole description, and which skills their parent already read. Before, a worker was sent no skills at all and the list had no address. (D566; Closes #2036) |
| 0.981.0 | 2026-11-07 | This change combines HB-utf8-tools, HA-EDIT-typed, HA-EDIT-blocks, HA-EDIT-readargs, HA-EDIT-anchor, and HA-EDIT-mvread. Hashline parsing denies byte-indexed string slices in yi-tools and uses character-boundary-safe access throughout its tokenizer, input recovery, parser, metadata handling, and multiline grep numbering. Patch failures retain their existing display text while gaining typed variants for callers. Reclaim recognizes only bracketed path headers that carry a snapshot tag. Block resolution covers multiline Python signatures, including single-quoted defaults, and Rust lifetimes without changing one-line definitions or character literals. Read handles existing and tagged bracketed paths before glob routing, validates numeric string arguments, and refuses a zero limit or multiline find needle with its rule. Non-Rust block comments keep their delimiters out of block depth across line boundaries, and comment markers inside regular-expression classes do not swallow the rest of a line. Shell scripts, makefiles and shell dotfiles such as `.bashrc` or `.profile` keep plain brace scanning, so a glob like `/var/backups/*` does not open a block comment; an extensionless file takes that scan only when it is one of those names or its shebang names a shell, and every other extensionless file — a `Jenkinsfile`, an `.eslintrc`, a `#!/usr/bin/env node` or `#!/usr/bin/env php` CLI — keeps C-style block-comment stripping. Ordinary unseen gap anchors were already refused before this change: the parent collects a gap anchor and the unseen-line guard refuses it. The new behaviour is the clipped-row carve-out alone, and `hashline_patcher::a_clipped_row_anchors_an_adjacent_insert_but_not_its_replacement` is the test that proves it. `hashline_patcher::unseen_gap_anchors_are_refused_without_changing_the_file` guards only that the carve-out leaves ordinary unseen gap anchors refused; it passes on the parent too, so it is a regression guard and not a red proof. Only displayed clipped rows can anchor adjacent inserts and register pastes without their full text; every replacement still requires shown lines. Addressable line counts exclude the trailing split artifact, moves refuse to overwrite an existing destination, and unreadable files report their actual read failure instead of inviting an overwrite. (Closes #1502, Closes #136, Refs #804, Refs #807, Refs #1442) |
| 0.980.0 | 2026-11-07 | The kernel's environment was fixed when it booted, so a plugin installed by `yi plugin add` mid-session reached the bash tool and `rlm.bash` (which build PATH per command) but not `!` lines, `%%bash` or `subprocess` in a cell. Before each cell the kernel service compares the installed program plugins with the directories it last put on the kernel's PATH and, when they differ, runs an internal cell that leads PATH with the installed directories and drops the ones a `yi plugin remove` took away, leaving every other entry alone, so a directory a cell added to PATH survives the refresh and a removal reaches the kernel instead of waiting for a restart. A kernel whose plugin set has not changed since boot is left alone. The journey test `kernel_lane::a_plugin_added_after_the_kernel_booted_is_on_the_next_cells_path` boots a kernel, seeds a PATH entry from a cell, publishes a plugin program, finds it with `!which` and `subprocess`, and checks the entry and the removal both landed on the kernel's PATH. (Closes #1202) |
| 0.979.0 | 2026-11-07 | The review bot reads a head once. Until now a draft needed a second clean round, so the review job called `just pr review` twice and the second call paid for a full second read of a head that had not moved: #1977 and #1974 ran two clean rounds on one sha minutes apart, #1830 three, #1888 three with a void between them that cost $0.51 and a $1.09 round that asked every lens although round 3 had lost only one. One clean round on the head now takes a draft out of draft and lets a PR merge, `just pr review` refuses a head a round read whole, clean or blocked, whether the trigger is a reopen, the job's second call, a sweep or `--again`, and only a new head earns a new round. A round that lost a lens is finished alone: the retry asks only the lenses still owed, a void in between no longer makes it forget which, and the findings the first round kept stand in the round that finishes the head instead of going through three refuters each again. `--again` now goes only past the retry cap of such a head, and the scheduled sweep leaves a head alone while its own review job is still pending. `.ruler/097-landing.md`, the yi-forge and pr-review skills and docs/FORGE.md say one round. (D565; Closes #2067) |
| 0.978.0 | 2026-11-07 | A 2,000-line edit paid the same line diff four times: once for the rendered patch, once for the changed lines, once for the line map, and once for the ripwire regions. `EditScript` is now built once per `(before, after)` pair, carried on the section's result, and read by all four, so the quadratic table is computed once. A snapshot's text is an `Arc<str>`, so the clones an edit's reply makes are pointers, and the snapshot lock is released before the syntax checker spawns instead of being held across it. A multiline grep numbered each hit by counting newlines from the file's start, which made a 4 MB search over about 15,000 matches scan tens of gigabytes; the walk between matches is one pass now and the numbers are unchanged. `rustfmt` is checked on the file's text over stdin rather than by path, because a path makes it follow the root's `mod` declarations and read, and fail on, a child file the edit did not touch. Session lookup no longer keeps a repository-discovery miss, so a worktree created later in the same process is included without a restart. A grep hit on a line past 512 characters was cut with `…` and nothing said so, so the model saw a hit whose matched text was gone and no way back to it. The page now ends with the same row `read` writes for a clipped line: how many lines were cut, the 512-character cap, and the `sed -n` call that prints them whole. `get_context`'s header counted all six layers whether or not the call asked for them, so a plain call opened `PARTIAL` and read as a broken packet; it counts the layers asked for, and a layer it did not ask for says `not asked:` instead of `absent:`. The same packet's file skeletons now show declarations one indent level in, so an `impl`'s methods and a class's `def`s reach the model instead of only the block that opens them. Five helpers that each existed twice are one each: the goal check, plan verify's checker and the `exec://` adapter share one shell-check builder that takes the environment policy, the deadline and the stop flag; `content_hash` is `fetch`'s one implementation; `KernelServiceMap::read` and `dump` share their prologue; the two kernel variable cells share their namespace lookup; and hashline's two path unquoters are one function. (Closes #1532) |
| 0.977.0 | 2026-11-07 | The branch guard now refuses direct feature-ledger edits from older pull requests too. All ledger additions and edits travel through the existing change-file operations, which the recorder applies on main; measurement found no hunk class that needs a new recorder operation. Seen red: `scripts/record_changes.py::ledger_selfcheck` commits a direct feature-ledger edit on the `old-edit` scratch branch at 2026-10-20, then calls `refuses("an older direct edit", ...)`. Against the pre-change guard, `python3 scripts/record_changes.py --selfcheck` failed with `an older direct edit read 0`; the fixed guard refuses the edit with a `ledger~:` or `ledger+:` repair line. The fixture is the recorder's real twelve-file landing history at `PIN`. (D564; Closes #2008) |
| 0.976.0 | 2026-11-07 | A live committed message carries the newest active-main entries after the prior commit cursor, oldest first, alongside the saved message.
