The suffix uses the replay entry and byte limits, and a cut notice names the kept and total counts and the resume call that returns the complete branch. The console appends those ancestors before the message while retaining replay/live deduplication, so hidden ModelChange and ToolResult entries keep the visible tool call on the active path and Up/Enter rewind to that selected call. (Refs #1442) |
| 0.975.0 | 2026-11-07 | `yi ask --scope GLOB`, repeatable, confines a run's writes inside its working tree to the paths the globs name. Two Kimi fix passes on eval-only PRs (#1630, #1638) added runtime lock edits and a test in crates/runtime to turn a CI-red test green; the brief named what to touch and nothing held them to it. A write the scope does not name is denied in every mode, yolo included, and the result names the scope and the path. Judged: `write`, `edit` (every section and `MV` destination), `grep` with `apply`, and the unquoted redirects of a bash command, read as a shell reads them, so a `->` or `=>` in a heredoc body, a comment or a quoted string names no file. A path is named when it or a directory above it matches (`*` stops at a `/`, `**` does not), a link out of the scope is judged where it leads, and a path outside the working tree is left to the sandbox. The scope is a kept command rule that is never journaled, so a child inherits it, a walled one too, and `--continue` opens unscoped. A glob that is absolute, empty or malformed exits 2 before the model runs, and `--scope` on any verb but `ask` is refused. A run that ends on a scope refusal exits 4 like any headless denial. A redirect into scratch inside the tree, a log under `target/`, is judged like any write and wants a scope of its own. Not judged: what a program writes (`sed -i`, `tee`, `cp`, `mv`, `rm`, a build, a kernel cell), a redirect inside quotes or built by expansion, and an absolute path in a lane child's own worktree, whose broker keeps its parent's tree. A relative redirect after a `cd`, `pushd` or `popd` is refused: the shell's directory is never interpreted, so no relative target after one can be proven to be inside the brief, and a subshell's change counts too (over-refusal, never a hole the other way). The word is assembled as a shell assembles it, quotes and escapes folded into it, and read where a shell would run one: behind a keyword (`if`, `then`, `elif`, `else`, `while`, `until`, `do`, `{`, `!`, `builtin`, `command`, `time`) as much as at a command's start, so `%cd`, `-cd` and `grep cd` are not a directory change while `'cd'`, `c"d"` and `c\d` are. That refusal has no wider `--scope` remedy and the message says so: the redirect needs an absolute path, or the `cd` moved out of the command. An absolute redirect target is judged as written in every command. No `permissions.scope` key: a standing scope would confine every run, not the one a brief names. (D563; Closes #1674) |
| 0.974.0 | 2026-11-07 | The merge queue now reads its own base and retry markers, the last ones in the candidate tip's body, and refuses a base marker that is not an ancestor of the tip in favour of the first-parent or merge-base fallback. The unused split counter is gone, and the batch, conflict and green scenarios in the queue's self-test now drive the tick's real observer instead of a copy of it. (Closes #2027) |
| 0.973.0 | 2026-11-07 | A model gateway that speaks the OpenAI chat-completions API is now a provider of its own. `providers.<id>` in `~/.yi/config.json` speaks chat completions and names its `baseUrl`, where its key is (`apiKeyEnv`, else a literal `apiKey` for a gateway placeholder that is not a secret, else the yi-oauth token at `~/.yi/providers/tokens/<id>.json`) and its `models`, each needing only an `id`, with `name`, `reasoning`, `input`, `contextWindow`, `maxTokens` and `cost` optional. A model of one is addressed `<id>/<model>` like any other. The declared models merge after the bundled catalog and the refreshed cache, so `yi catalog refresh` neither rewrites nor drops them and never fetches a custom provider. Requests go through the OpenAI completions adapter at the declared base URL with plain `max_tokens` and none of OpenAI's own fields. An id a built-in provider, `laya` or `faux` already holds refuses when the config loads, naming the id. A provider with no key anywhere is refused before any request, naming the variable that would supply one; a real key belongs in that variable, never in `config.json`. The block is read once per process, so the settings row says an edit takes hold after a restart. `yi doctor` has a `providers` row naming each one, its model count and base URL, and where its key resolved, naming the token file a store key came from. (D562; Closes #1816) |
| 0.972.0 | 2026-11-07 | Reclaim no longer cuts the newest read of a file that a kernel cell edited through `yi.tools.edit` or `yi.tools.write` (Refs #1242). Before this, only the model's own `edit` and `write` results marked a file as edited. A program's edit appeared only in its cell's `ipython` result, in the `calls` rows, so the read whose line tags the next edit needs was cut once it aged, like any other read. Reclaim now reads those rows the same way compaction's modified files and the todo landed count do. Rows for edits that failed do not protect a read. (D561) |
| 0.971.0 | 2026-11-07 | A tree whose per-call snapshots turn off now spends the cut notice once: the next mutating call's tool result carries the row. (Refs #1940) |
| 0.970.0 | 2026-11-07 | E-cli-doctor: `yi doctor` now reads the user's config leniently before the strict global load, so its lane check honors `lanes.slots` even though a malformed config remains a doctor row rather than a fatal error. The daemon socket and ledger checks honor `--socket` instead of always reading the default path, and the socket row judges that the file it would unlink is a socket before `--fix` removes it: a path that names something else is reported and left alone. E-slow-exec-probe: exec-tool schema probes now run side by side under a pool of four, each with its own two-second deadline measured from its own start, so one slow tool cannot skip another: K bad executables cost 2s x ceil(K/4) during discovery with up to four child processes alive at once, rather than 2s each in turn, and no directory of executables can fork whole at every session start. Discovery returns both loaded tools and skipped files with reasons, and a probe that panics is named as skipped rather than dropped. `yi doctor` gained an `exec-tools` row naming every skipped executable and its timeout, nonzero exit, or invalid schema. No decisions are claimed. (Closes #1515, Refs #1442) |
| 0.969.0 | 2026-11-07 | `just test`, `just journeys`, and `just dist-suite` now run tests through `scripts/test_tmp.sh`. Nextest gives each test process a private `TMPDIR`; each Cargo-only step has one per invocation. The wrapper creates that directory under the inherited `TMPDIR` (or `/tmp` when unset) and removes it on exit, including on a kill. `scripts/test_tmp_test.py` runs in `just test` and checks a real nextest process and the EXIT trap after SIGTERM. A full suite into an empty TMPDIR left 171 entries before and 0 after. Merge note: `scripts/guardrails/baselines/binary_size_budget_linux.json` remains removed, matching main's removal of the binary-size gate. (D560) |
| 0.968.0 | 2026-11-07 | `yi worker replace --binary <path>` swaps one root's running `yi acp` worker for a freshly built binary while the supervisor, its clients and every session's durable state stay where they are: the console keeps its pane, and the same session resumes from the ledger with its plan, mail, claims and kernel variables intact. The supervisor keeps a generation per root, a map of the binary each root was last replaced with, and one swap in flight. The replacement asks the old worker for an inventory first (`_yi/prepare_replace`): a turn in flight, a queued prompt, an unread follow-up, a permission ask, a running child, a running job, a kernel cell, or a kernel whose namespace its snapshot cannot hold whole refuses the swap by name, and nothing is replaced. An idle worker flushes every kernel through its checked dispose, asks its inventory again, and answers `prepared`; only the supervisor's acceptance retires it, by closing its stdin, so a late answer to a refused swap leaves it serving; the supervisor spawns the requested binary in its place, restores the sessions the root owned (`_yi/restore`, no replay), tells every attached client to resume (`_yi/worker_replaced`), and only then answers the verb. A worker that does not answer within 30 s is refused; one that does not close is killed at the same deadline rather than left serving a build the caller already replaced; a root whose sessions do not restore within 60 s is reported failed rather than successful. The generation is what keeps the old build from coming back: a close from a superseded generation is ignored, and the binary a root was last replaced with outranks `current_exe` when that root's worker is later spawned again. Every client request bound for a root between two builds is refused with the replacement's id and the generation pair instead of being dropped. `just dev` rebuilds and then calls the verb, so a dev loop no longer kills `yi serve`; a daemon too old to know the verb says so in its own refusal. Async methods (`_yi/prepare_replace`) are answered from the worker's request loop, and the final kernel flush is awaited before the answer, so a variable cannot be lost to a replacement that already reported success. Deliberate ceilings of this first version: a replacement never migrates a live turn, a job or a pending ask (they are refused, never carried); a session whose mailbox or scheduler was bound in the old worker is re-bound from the ledger by `_yi/restore` but its next wake is not proven against a missing durable subscription; the kernel's event log post-mortem keeps only the last 200 events per variable, unchanged by this landing. (Closes #1707) |
| 0.967.0 | 2026-11-07 | The connection reuse test now holds every reply until eight requests are waiting, so its "eight concurrent streams" really are open together. On a loaded queue runner one stream used to finish and lend its connection before the others dialed, so the test counted six or seven accepts and ejected merge-queue candidates that had nothing to do with it. (Closes #2056) |
| 0.966.0 | 2026-11-07 | The runtime's two remaining silent cuts are named (unit CAPS-runtime, finishing what 0.753.0 left open on #1487). A `rlm.find_models` search that matched more than `limit` already produced a `[… kept of total matching models (limit N); the rest: await rlm.find_models("", limit=T)]` row in the host reply, and the `rlm` shim read only `models`, so the row never reached a cell and the model saw a short list with nothing to say it was short. The shim prints the row now, and its docstring says the list it returns is at most the `limit` asked for. A variable the snapshot left out came back as silence. The snapshot has always counted its skips (`KernelSnapshotResult.skipped`: over the 16 MiB per-variable cap, over the aggregate cap, or unpicklable), but only the snapshot's own marker line saw them; the resume read an index that no longer held the name and said nothing, so a revived kernel's notice claimed a namespace it did not have. The skip list now rides the index itself under a `_skips` key — the loops skip every name starting with `_`, so it cannot collide with a variable, and one write commits the names and the skips together instead of a second file that could describe a snapshot that never landed. The restore normalises each entry to `{name, reason}` with a string reason, so a stored shape cannot make the marker line undecodable and blank the revive notice, then appends those names to the `failed` names it reports. The notice names each one, cut at `RESTORE_FAILED_SHOWN`, and always ends by naming `_yi_restore_failures`, the restored kernel list that holds every failure with its reason; the cut row's continuation is that bare name, so no builtin a user shadowed is resolved. No type changed: `KernelSnapshotSkip` already says "one name the snapshot/restore helpers could not process, with a short reason", so a name the snapshot skipped is a name restore did not produce. Risk and rollback: the restore reads no new file; the skip list is a `_skips` key inside `kernel-state.dill`, and the notice prints the names without their reasons, pointing at `_yi_restore_failures` for those. A binary from before this change reads that key as a blob, so its first resume names a phantom `_skips` (TypeError) and loses the real skip list. Delete `kernel-state.dill` before reverting, or accept that one report until the reverted binary writes its next snapshot, which drops the key; a snapshot taken before reverting does not help, since this version always writes `_skips`. (Closes #1487) |
| 0.965.0 | 2026-11-07 | A route's cache marks now follow what its own session shows (Refs #748). Two behaviours build on the ledger fold D315 landed, and neither asks a model nor changes provider routing. The tail mark: a request's own tail is where the next request reads from, and a route that wrote it and read nothing back for four consecutive unexplained requests is writing a mark nothing reads. The fold stops the moving tail on that route for an hour and keeps every mark before it, the previous tail included, since those are what the next request does read. Requests during the suspension do not re-arm it. After the hour the mark is written once more: four more zero-read writes suspend it again, a read ends the run and the mark stays. The refusal: a provider that does not know a cache field answers 400 rather than ignoring it. A 400 whose body names one of `cache_control`, `cache_ttl`, `prompt_cache_key`, `prompt_cache_retention`, `session_id` or `cache` is asked again once with only the cache fields the encoder wrote removed from the body. Fields inside tool schemas, tool calls and tool results stay unchanged, and the reply records the refused field as a `cache_stripped` diagnostic. A 400 that names none of them is reported unchanged, never resent. The previous tail needed no switch of its own: D295's build already sends it on every loop request, so the escalation it was meant to gate is unconditional. The TTL-expiry escalation ladder remains open under #748. This PR keeps D315's cost-minimising TTL choice and does not close that issue. (D559) |
| 0.964.0 | 2026-11-07 | A child's `kernel://main/<var>` read queues behind the parent's running cell and answers `Busy` after 5 s, exactly when the orchestrate protocol tells children to read it. IPython runs one execute at a time, so the read cannot be served live. `VariableReadError::Busy` now names the route that works: the agent `rlm.put`s the value before it awaits, a member with a kernel reads it back with `rlm.get`, and a reader with no kernel takes a computed value through `context_keys` (`family://<name>` is the put's sidecar, not the object). The orchestrate prompt says the live read needs an idle parent. (Closes #1554) |
| 0.963.0 | 2026-11-07 | A worker (`rlm.run(role="worker")`) may now name `ipython` in `tools=`, which boots it a kernel of its own at worker prices; it is allowed, not a default, so a bare worker is unchanged. Root and worker sessions share `attach_kernel` and the fetch, history and memory registration; the worker has no spawn or messaging handlers, so a cell in a worker cannot spawn. Its `rlm.fetch` reads live values such as `kernel://main/<var>` through its own resolver. `reader::session` is the one admission check and builds the kernel only when the worker keeps `ipython`, so a one-turn worker builds none. A walled worker where the kernel would run unconfined (no Seatbelt) keeps the loud refusal, `WALLED_KERNEL_UNCONFINED`. The proofs are `kernel_across_sessions::a_worker_fetches_a_parent_kernel_value`, `kernel_across_sessions::a_walled_worker_naming_ipython_keeps_the_kernel_boundary`, and `reader_e2e::a_one_turn_worker_naming_ipython_builds_no_kernel`. (Closes #1584) |
| 0.962.0 | 2026-11-07 | Reclaim decides a cut in tokens on every route. The price test (`tokens dropped × cache-read price × requests` against `tokens after the first cut × (write − read price)`) left a route whose catalog prices cache at $0 (a subscription such as the Kimi lanes) unable to ever cut, since its read price was zero. A cut now pays when the tokens it drops, times the requests so far, exceed ten reads of the tokens after the first cut result (`REWRITE_READS`, a constant, not a setting), and a batch must drop at least 2,000 tokens (`MIN_DROPPED`, unchanged). A lapsed cache still cuts at once at the same floor. Dollar rates no longer enter the rule; the stored session is untouched. The simulator run over the newest lane sessions and the Kimi lane A/B with `yi catalog usage kimi` before and after the cut need the network and the lane's credentials, and are not part of this change: they are the gate the issue names and are still owed. (D558; Refs #1762) |
| 0.961.0 | 2026-11-07 | Review correction: the Seen red entry for `every_result_kind_survives_an_empty_and_a_control_ridden_value` is withdrawn. That test guards control-character sanitization that predates this change, and its single-line fixture cannot produce the newline-picture failure the entry described. The new `a_result_value_that_looks_like_a_cap_notice_stays_value` regression uses a hand-written value whose last row begins `[…]`; against the unfixed predicate it failed with `the value's own row was not rendered as markdown`, because that row had become the pane's notice. A notebook result was drawn as plain text whatever the kernel sent. The value's display bundle was read for its `text/plain` member alone, so a `text/markdown`, `application/json` or `text/x-python` representation was discarded before the pane could see it. The kernel now keeps the richest textual member of the bundle and names it beside the result (`result_mime` on `ExecuteResult`, `resultMime` in the cell's details), and the pane dispatches on that name through one table: markdown goes through the chat's own renderer, JSON is pretty-printed and highlighted and collapses past the 20-row cap with the same `[…]` row every other cut speaks, python is highlighted, and a mime no row names draws the text it already was. A bundle that declares JSON it cannot parse says so in one row instead of pretending to be a tree, and one Yi's own byte cap cut says the cap rather than claiming the value was never JSON. The mimes the kernel ranks by live in one const beside `ExecuteResult`, and the console's renderer table is keyed by the named consts, so a reorder cannot remap a renderer. The cell's result is that member for the model too (markdown source or re-serialized JSON, not the `text/plain` repr), which is deliberate: the repr of a markdown display is an object address. A member too large for the cell's char cap falls back to `text/plain` where the value has one, and with none the kernel's `[…]` cut row speaks. The byte-cap notice on a markdown result draws as its own muted row, not inside a table or fence. Sessions recorded before this carry no mime and draw exactly as they did. The size raises are the price of six contract tests at each crate's own seam plus the renderer itself: the pane's tests live in `crates/console/src/panes.rs` and `crates/console/src/app/notebook.rs`, the kernel's in `crates/kernel/tests/reduce.rs`, and their fixtures are the recorded sessions already in `crates/tui/tests/fixtures/sessions` (a 6,934-character markdown assessment and one kernel result's details). What was weighed and deleted first: `highlighted` merged into `capped` as a language argument, the four per-kind cap paths folded into `cap_rows`, the console's duplicate mime list replaced by `RESULT_MIMES`, and a two-line doc comment in `crates/kernel/src/client.rs` closed onto one line to keep that file at its 1,000-line ceiling. (Closes #1740) |
| 0.960.0 | 2026-11-07 | A run no longer dies on the wait for its first byte without being told what to change. `Model` gains an optional `readTimeoutSecs`, and the stream request takes its read deadline from the model rather than a constant in `yi-ai`: the bundled, cached and custom catalogs all reach it through the same serde shape, so an entry written by hand or by a cache refresh carries it with no other change. The deadline bounds one read, so a generation that keeps emitting chunks outlives its value and only a silence longer than it does not. A read timeout is neither retried by `send_with_retry` nor resent by `pump_sse_with_resend`, because either layer doubled a stall the field had already bounded; the refusal says it timed out reading response after N s, names `readTimeoutSecs` and its 60 s default, points at the catalog entry, and states how many attempts ran. A `yi ask` run that ends on a provider error now exits 1 in `--json` mode too, where it used to exit 0 with the error only in the final line. The loop does not re-run a turn that ended on that refusal, so one stall costs one deadline. A refresh reads the cached entry through the catalog overlay, so a `readTimeoutSecs` set by hand survives it, and a cursor-agent entry carrying the field is refused at load rather than accepted and dropped: that wire waits on its own connect and header bounds. (D557; Closes #1824) |
| 0.959.0 | 2026-11-07 | The contributor guide now walks a GitHub contributor from fork to draft pull request with plain git and GitHub, since the forge's push and PR recipes do not work from a mirror clone. It says what to send the owner first, that the pinned email must match case and all, which tools the recipes need, how to check every commit's signature, identity and whitespace before pushing, the caps, paths, dependency edits and added-line content the importer refuses, that the owner supplies the issue number and runs intake, and that some guardrails fail on a mirror clone by design. (Refs #1719) |
| 0.958.0 | 2026-11-07 | `yi stats` now counts how the model uses skills. One session's view and the new `yi stats --by skills` fold report skill reads only after durable `custom{fetch}` entries prove the resolver served them, every `Relevant:` pointer including the cap's extras, and how many of those a read of the same skill followed within 3 assistant turns. Fork-seeded reminder copies count once, and the review bot's footer uses the same served-read rule. Before, the 0-of-62 baseline for followed pointers came from a hand scan of the session files, and nothing could show whether the coming start step changes it. ## Seen red - `rollup::every_address_in_a_capped_reminder_counts_and_can_be_followed`: limiting `skill_names` to one address produced `[1, 1, 0]` instead of `[1, 2, 1]`; the fixture is a synthetic reminder with one visible and one capped address, then a fetch of the capped skill. - `rollup::a_filesystem_skill_read_counts_only_when_its_local_fetch_arrives`: changing the fetch rule from `local://` to `file://` produced `[0, 1, 0]` instead of `[1, 1, 1]`; the fixture is a synthetic pointer, attempted filesystem read and local `SKILL.md` fetch. - `rollup::a_reminder_copied_into_a_fork_counts_once`: disabling the reminder dedupe guard produced `[0, 2, 0]` instead of `[0, 1, 0]`; the fixture writes one synthetic reminder into parent and child session files under the recorded root fixture's header. - `rollup::tests::only_skill_fetches_are_retained_for_dedupe`: before irrelevant fetches were excluded, `local://src/lib.rs` was retained (`left: true`, `right: false`); the fixture is three synthetic fetch entries covering an ordinary file, a local skill and a catalog skill. (Closes #2038) |
| 0.957.0 | 2026-11-07 | A child's token lease was drawn from its parent and never held: `rlm.run(tokens=N)` reserved the number, the parent was billed for it, and nothing stopped the child at it — the brief even said so, "(reserved, not enforced)". Worse, the count it was billed for was `usage.total_tokens`, so a child re-sending one 15k cached prefix was charged 15k on every reply; a twelve-turn reader crossed a 1000-token lease it had spent 1200 of. Both halves are one fact, a child's spend, so they are fixed at one seam. `ChildRecord::charge` folds `Usage::fresh` — input plus cache write plus output, the cache read excluded — into the count, and the record ends the run at the first reply whose fold leaves the lease spent: it aborts the session and `SubagentHost::lease_ending` names the ending `Failed { class: LeaseSpent }` (additive; an older host decodes it as `Other`), with a notice that quotes the cap. A leased ending whose crossing is not yet latched refolds the transcript clone already collected for conclusion, outside the family lock, so a final reply cannot race the watcher. Unleased and already latched endings do not refold, and a spent lease refuses follow-up work rather than running another turn unpriced. The brief line now states the cap it enforces, and a child at exactly its lease runs one more reply: the check is `>`, not `>=`. (D556; Closes #1567) |
| 0.956.0 | 2026-11-07 | `just push` now installs `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` when rustup lacks it, then runs clippy for the branch's changed crates with all targets and warnings as errors, plus the `yi-cli` no-default-features configuration. A diff without crate paths lints the workspace. A machine without rustup or cargo, or one whose target install fails, prints a named skip and can still push. The postmerge recorder on main now runs the existing dist-size ratchet after recording change files. A Linux binary measured beyond its stored budget gets its own `Ratchet: linux dist X -> Y, as the forge measured it` commit before main is pushed, so later branches do not inherit that size drift. The record job uploads a bundle of the new commits. A separate push job downloads that artifact without checking out landed code and pushes from a fresh clone with hooks, filesystem monitoring and inherited Git configuration disabled, so build code cannot reach the main-writing token or control its push. A failed ratchet still lets the recording commit push, then fails the push job. (Closes #2014) |
| 0.955.0 | 2026-11-07 | The memory block is the one thing every session pays for before it reads a word of the request. It was rendered at session start and never again, so a session asking about lanes and a session asking about the markdown renderer carried byte-identical text: the header, the counts and one index line per note, capped at 200 lines or 25,000 bytes per store. On this machine 193 recorded sessions carry that fence, median 1,339 bytes (335 tokens by the `bytes/4` rule the eval uses); the fixture store of 16 notes, which is what a real store looks like after a few months, costs 3,574 bytes and 885 tokens a session, 163 of its 176 injected note-lines irrelevant to the letters the case is about. Session start now only reconciles the stores, counts a fresh session and journals the summary. The first prompt reaches the extension host before the first request, which is the one moment the block can still be attached without touching the frozen prefix (D310), so `MemoryExt` answers `PromptSubmitted`: it scores every note with the store's own BM25, weights the types (`feedback` 1.5, `project` 1.4, `user` 1.25, `reference` 1.0), keeps a note whose hook restates a higher-ranked one as an index row without a body, and spends the unranked index's row bytes (never less than two body caps) on the bodies of at most three scored notes before the remaining index lines. A blank first prompt spends nothing, and a resumed session keeps the ranked block restored from its snapshot. Store failures and load caps stay named, and every cut says what it dropped and how to get it. The eval is `evals/memory_inject.py` over `evals/fixtures/memory-inject`: eleven real first messages from `~/.yi/sessions`, hand-labelled against a sixteen-note fixture store with a reason per label, scored on both arms. The before arm is `before/block.txt`, rendered by the pre-change build and committed as a fixture; the after arm is rendered by `crates/runtime/tests/memory_inject.rs` through `memory::focused` and scored by the same script, so the replay spends nothing and rides `just check`. The one-off before fixture was regenerated on the pre-change build with `MI_BEFORE_OUT=evals/fixtures/memory-inject/before/block.txt cargo test -p yi-runtime --test integration memory_inject::dump_before_blocks -- --exact`; that generator is not shipped as a permanently skipping test. Run 0088 in docs/eval-ledger.md: recall@body 0.67 against 0.00, precision@body 0.25 against 0.00, 9,462 tokens against 9,735, and 100 irrelevant note-lines against 163 (24 of them carrying a body). The paid judge run (`label --model openrouter/… --cap-usd 10`) is written but has not run: this session had no network, so the labels are the owner's agent answering the same four questions the judge prompt asks, and the ledger row says so. A resume now distinguishes a snapshot that restored the memory yard from one that restored none: only the former spends the first-prompt latch, so a Pi-format or snapshot-less session earns the same ranked block as a fresh session. Repository-store notes have direct body and count coverage. Near-duplicate hooks are tokenized once per candidate, body cuts use `yi_context::fit`, and the eval stages through the shared `Scratch` cleanup harness. The paid judge writes its completed cases to `labels-judge.json`, leaves `labels.json` byte-identical, omits unjudged cases at the cap, and refuses any reply without a numeric `usage.cost`. The gate runs the judge tests from `evals/tests/test_memory_inject.py`; `replay` and `check` both accept `--labels`, so the judged arm is scored without copying it over the hand-labelled fixture. (D555; Closes #1860) |
| 0.954.0 | 2026-11-06 | A running tool card no longer reserves blank rows for the result it has not received. The card is its header alone until output arrives, then grows one row at a time as the result streams in, and a finished card ends on its last result row instead of padding to four. This replaces the bounded collapsed-card reservation of D516, which held the card's height to spare the screen a jump when the result landed. (D554) |
| 0.953.0 | 2026-11-06 | A compaction summary now carries the values that sat only in tool results. The summarizer prompt (both the first and the update form) asks for a `## Values` section of exact identifiers, paths, numbers, hashes and error strings, including values still visible in the kept tail, and a `## Dropped` section listing what the summary left out and where to read it back. After the summarizer replies, `yi_context::keep_values` checks Values and Dropped independently: a reply missing either section gets the previous summary's newest rows back, up to `VALUES_CARRIED_CAP` or `DROPPED_CARRIED_CAP`. A cut row at the point of the cut names the numeric cap and the `history://self` grep that reads the rest. A reply that has the section is trusted, so a corrected value replaces the old one. The stored session ledger is unchanged; a cut stays a view over it (`compaction_faux::a_second_compaction_keeps_the_values_the_first_recorded`, `primitives::a_summary_that_lost_its_values_section_gets_the_previous_rows_back`, `primitives::a_summary_that_lost_its_dropped_section_gets_the_previous_rows_back`, `primitives::carried_values_past_the_cap_say_how_many_were_left_out`, `primitives::carried_dropped_rows_past_the_cap_keep_the_newest_and_name_the_cut`). Seen red, with each fix disabled on this head: - `primitives::a_summary_that_lost_its_values_section_gets_the_previous_rows_back` panicked at `crates/context/tests/primitives.rs:1250:5` because the previous build id was absent; the fixture is the inline `FIRST_SUMMARY` plus a second summary with no Dropped section. - `primitives::carried_values_past_the_cap_say_how_many_were_left_out` panicked at `crates/context/tests/primitives.rs:1304:5` because no cap notice appeared; the fixture is 43 generated value rows, which exercises the cap at 40 plus three. - `primitives::carried_dropped_rows_past_the_cap_keep_the_newest_and_name_the_cut` panicked at `crates/context/tests/primitives.rs:1330:5` with only the current Values section left; the fixture is 43 generated dropped rows with history addresses. - `compaction_faux::a_second_compaction_keeps_the_values_the_first_recorded` panicked at `crates/runtime/tests/compaction_faux.rs:920:5` because the second summary lost build id `7f3a9c21`; the fixture is the faux provider's two scripted summaries. - `compaction_faux::a_kept_tail_over_the_ceiling_compacts_once_not_on_every_request` panicked at `crates/runtime/tests/compaction_faux.rs:715:5` with `5 compactions in ten requests`; the fixture is a faux session whose 8,000-character opening remains over the 2,010-token ceiling. - `compaction_faux::a_summary_quote_the_addressed_message_lacks_is_marked_unverified` panicked at `crates/runtime/tests/compaction_faux.rs:1040:5` with the fabricated `user://2` quote left unmarked; the fixture is the faux summarizer's planted quote beside one verbatim quote. - `primitives::a_span_stored_without_a_count_is_never_summed_as_zero` panicked at `crates/context/tests/primitives.rs:1064:5` with `1 of 4 brief lines` instead of `1 of 4 or more brief lines`; the fixture is a stored view holding one legacy span without a count beside one three-line span. - `primitives::a_demoted_brief_names_what_was_kept_the_cap_and_the_read_that_restores_it` panicked at `crates/context/tests/primitives.rs:1013:5` because the Earlier section had no kept/total, cap, or restore read; the fixture is 125 generated read results, then repeated 121-line rounds past `EARLIER_CAP`. (Refs #1760) |
| 0.952.0 | 2026-11-06 | An edit answered with its whole changed region, every line plus three either side, and never with a line it dropped. A twelve-row edit cost about 1.5x the body it was given, and the guide's own `range sized to new content` mistake, a `PUT N.=M:` whose range runs past the content the body actually replaces, took a live row out of the file with nothing in the reply to show it had happened. The reply is now `updated; +A -R at line N`, the rows the edit removed (`-before N:TEXT`, each clipped to 120 columns, at most 8, the ninth landing a `[… 8 of N removed rows (EDIT_REMOVED_CAP 8); the rest: …]` row naming the read that shows what the file holds now) and the new tag. The rows the model authored stay anchors under that tag without being reprinted, and so do the rows it had already seen, so a follow-up edit on an unprinted line still lands without a re-read. (Closes #1522) |
| 0.951.0 | 2026-11-06 | The dist binary size gate is gone, reversing D249/D424's measured ratchet and hard cap. The Linux ratchet failed merge-queue candidates whenever a batch grew the binary past 16 KiB jitter; #1891 alone measured 14,334,344 bytes against 14,290,184 on three candidates in a row, and every growing PR needed its own baseline-only commit. The guardrail, baselines, CI measurement and automatic ratchets are removed. The startup budget stays, with local full guardrails still building the dist binary for it; CI skips that build because it never times startup. Historical measurements remain in the size ledger. (D553; Closes #2050) |
| 0.950.0 | 2026-11-06 | A justfile was the one cheap source of tools every repository already had, and reaching it cost a `bash` call the model had to spell from memory: `yi tool import just` now dumps the project's recipes with just's own `--dump --dump-format json` and writes one exec-tool wrapper per recipe into `~/.yi/tools`, the directory `session_tools` already scans. The schema is the recipe's real parameters — a default makes one optional, `*` and `+` become an array of strings — and the doc comment is the description, so the model reads the signature just enforces instead of guessing it. A call reaches the recipe as an argv array built from the JSON the exec-tool contract hands the wrapper, never through a shell string; the argument carrying a space and a quote arrives as one element, a literal default is sent in its own position, an omitted computed default is left for just to fill, and a later supplied value is refused rather than shifted into that gap. Every imported recipe stays an `exec` tool: a repository-authored doc marker cannot turn a shell body into an unprompted read. `yi: skip` and `[private]` leave a recipe out and are named in the run's output, and a recipe whose name is a built-in tool's is refused by that name before anything is written. Each wrapper's second line is a marker naming its justfile and recipe: a rerun reports `added`, `updated` and `unchanged`, deletes the wrappers it wrote for recipes the justfile dropped, refuses the whole import when another justfile holds a name, and never deletes or overwrites a hand-written tool or a file it cannot read as text. A wrapper for a recipe with parameters is `/bin/sh` around a `python3` one-liner that maps the call's JSON to argv, so it needs `python3` on PATH and says so by name when it is missing; a machine with only `uv` still imports, and the wrapper's own `--schema` path needs nothing. A recipe with no parameters needs no interpreter at all and execs `just` itself, which is the common `build`/`test`/`lint` case. The skill `tool-import` is where Yi is taught the command, the wrapper's schema and the skip marker, its catalog line carrying the usage. (D552; Closes #1877) |
| 0.949.0 | 2026-11-06 | A request mailed to a session that no process holds now starts it while `yi serve` runs (Closes #1246). The daemon polls `<sessions>/channels/` every two seconds, a poll with no new file-watch dependency that never reads the daemon ledger. A request that no subscription has read after a held session's own cadence plus a margin, and that is not past its deadline, makes the daemon spawn the worker for the session's repository (found from the session file's header) and resume the session with no replay and no model turn, which binds its mailbox so the letter is delivered as for any loaded session. A session started this way has no client to ask: a permission ask it raises is cancelled at once, and the requester receives a `failure` letter naming the refused action with `inReplyTo` set to its request, instead of the worker waiting on an answer nobody sends. The ask is refused whatever its kind; the classifier-backed approval the issue names for reviewable auto-mode asks is not part of this change, and a refusal is the fallback it names. Without a daemon nothing changes: the letter waits in the channel file with receipt `stored`. The journey `serve_daemon::a_request_in_a_stopped_sessions_mailbox_starts_it_and_is_read` writes a session and an old request on disk, starts the daemon and sees the letter acked. |
| 0.948.0 | 2026-11-06 | `yi ask --check CMD` (repeatable) makes a headless run answer for the work it claims to have finished. After each completed turn the command runs in the session's tool directory, though the goal loop's own check and off the runtime thread. A red one re-prompts the same session with that check's output through the view a goal check already produces, then streams the next turn. The loop lives in a new `crates/cli/src/ask_check.rs`. Every check is probed in the session's work directory before the session is recorded, so a refused run writes no session; `yi ask --check CMD` with no prompt claims the lane the run would and probes there, so both forms give one verdict. `main.rs` carries that prompt-less probe, the module line and the `work_dir` helper, which `build_session`, `shells.rs` and both probes share. A refused probe prints the same `error:` line and, under `--json`, the same `final` row as a refused prompted run. The loop releases an already-ended run before it starts a check. Each check's budget is the goal check's ten minutes cut to the time left before the run's deadline reserve, and only a green check returns the turn's success exit code. Every ending prints one `[…]` row on stderr and, under `--json`, one `{"type":"check"}` line: green, the 4th red run after 3 re-prompts, the deadline reserve, a run that ended on an error, a deadline or a refusal, and a check that could not spawn or timed out. A released red exits 4, and the run's leftovers print once after the last turn, never between them. No goal is created. ## Seen red - `ask_check::every_re_prompt_re_runs_the_check`: breaking to `Release::Cap` instead of running the check after a re-prompt produced `left: Some(4)`, `right: Some(0)` at `crates/cli/tests/ask_check.rs:149`; the faux cassette has two sentence-only turns followed by a `bash` turn. - `ask_check::a_check_that_stays_red_releases_at_the_cap`: raising the cap from three re-prompts to four produced `left: Some(1)`, `right: Some(4)` at `crates/cli/tests/ask_check.rs:185`; the faux cassette has four turns and the second check stays red because `done.txt` is never created. - `ask_check::a_check_never_holds_the_run_past_its_deadline`: replacing the remaining-deadline budget with the check's full ten minutes produced `left: Some(0)`, `right: Some(4)` at `crates/cli/tests/ask_check.rs:278`; two sentence-only faux turns run with `--deadline 2` and a `sleep 30` check. - `ask_check::a_check_that_passes_on_the_first_run_prompts_once`: releasing a green check as the cap produced `left: Some(4)`, `right: Some(0)` at `crates/cli/tests/ask_check.rs:241`; the faux cassette has one `bash` turn that creates `done.txt` before the check runs. (Closes #1528) |
| 0.947.0 | 2026-11-06 | The runtime's fifteen embedded prompts no longer ride the binary as raw text: `crates/runtime/build.rs` deflates each one and proves the round trip, `yi-runtime::packed::inflate` inflates it once on first use inside a `LazyLock<String>`, and every accessor reads through it. The dist binary measured 8,670,176 -> 8,637,152 bytes on this host, **-33,024 bytes**, two 16 KiB `__TEXT` pages back under the measured ratchet (8,686,704 + 16,384 jitter = 8,703,088: headroom 32,912 bytes before, 65,936 after), which sits under the 9 MiB hard cap of #1354; #1401's 8 MiB arithmetic and its "next 3.8 KB fails every build" are stale against that ratchet. `miniz_oxide` was already in the graph through `yi-ai`'s catalog and `yi-orb`, so nothing joins the dependency table and no new crate is linked. The deflate step is one `pack_bytes` function in `crates/runtime/pack.rs`, which the `yi-runtime`, `yi-ai`, `yi-kernel` and `yi-tui` build scripts `include!` in place of their own deflate-and-write pairs, so the model catalogs, Python runtime and logo masks gain the round-trip proof too. The `pack` wrapper in `crates/runtime/pack_file.rs` is shared by the two file-driven builds, `yi-runtime` and `yi-ai`; it refuses input that is not UTF-8, the check `include_str!` made at compile time. Each prompt static is one `packed!("stem")` line, and the graph inflates inside its one `OnceLock`. `every_packed_prompt_reads_as_its_own_file` holds all fourteen text prompts in one table and `the_shipped_graph_reads_as_its_own_file` checks the graph. They prove that each generated blob is a zlib stream shorter than its source, inflates to that source, and reaches its accessor unchanged. Each also requires its blob to have passed through `inflate`, so reverting an accessor to raw `include_str!` fails instead of passing with identical text. Seen red: `crates/runtime/src/packed.rs::tests::every_packed_prompt_reads_as_its_own_file`, before its rename, failed at `crates/runtime/src/packed.rs:126` with `not deflated from its own file` for all fourteen prompts after `inflate` was changed to hand the compressed bytes to `String::from_utf8`. The inputs were the production prompt static values and their source files, not a new fixture. Seen red for the accessor guard: reverting `IDENTITY` to `include_str!` failed `every_packed_prompt_reads_as_its_own_file` with `not deflated from its own file: ["identity"]`; restoring the graph's former direct decompression failed `the_shipped_graph_reads_as_its_own_file` with `shipped graph did not pass through inflate`. Both mutations used the production prompt files and their generated blobs. (Closes #1401) |
| 0.946.0 | 2026-11-06 | A child's answer is read one way: `rlm.result`, a reap and a plan finish all skip a chase turn's reply, so the reply a parent's `rlm.request` chased out of a child is never taken as the answer to its brief. A respawned service's first message is its incarnation header alone, since its kept transcript already opens with the brief, so the brief's bytes appear once in its history. `rlm.wait` no longer sends the one-release `updated` alias (read `changed`), and the child roster rows lose their always-null `active_session_id` and `session_id`. A final text over the body cap still goes whole to the family board, which every member can read, and its head now ends in the shared cut row naming bytes kept of total and `HEAD_BYTES`. The stuck check runs at most once a second per host whatever the number of waiters, and it latches through the same `StuckLatch` the plan timer uses. A HIGH discovery when the session has no goal reaches the parent as a note saying no completion gate holds on it, instead of failing the child's result with a `goal.create` refusal; an unreadable plan and a failed ledger write still hold the result back. The plan finish runs its red check and its discovery routing as two named steps. The plan test rig moved from `plan/dispatch.rs` to `plan/rig.rs`, built on `yi_ai::faux::faux_model`, which the CLI's `faux/faux-1` resolution now shares. (Closes #1529) |

| 0.944.0 | 2026-11-06 | The merge queue's candidate gate now runs from its own workflow, candidate.yml, which names the `queue` runner outright. The `runs-on` choice by branch that #2035 added never took effect: Forgejo evaluates `runs-on` with no `github` context, so every candidate job still landed on `ubuntu-latest`. pr.yml runs on pull requests only again, and the workflow check refuses a candidate.yml whose env or gate steps differ from pr.yml's, so the gate the queue lands on stays the one every PR passed. (Closes #2042) |
| 0.943.0 | 2026-11-06 | Yi's replies open with a status line. The `answer` tool's `summary`, which every answer already carried and no screen showed, now heads the reply as `Problem:`, `Ready:` or `Done:` and one plain sentence, a summary alone is a whole reply, and a `Done:` is sent back while a todo is blocked. A `Problem:` or `Ready:` line that a reply already on screen would hide is refused, so it is sent again over new text. JSON answers reach the programs that read them unchanged. The identity prompt is rewritten around how the owner wants to be answered: Yi does the work itself and stops only at what cannot be undone or speaks for the user, keeps facts apart from opinions, and matches the user's language, length and tone, in plain words with no code, file names or jargon unless the user writes them. Doctrine now lets a diagnosis end in its fix. The guardrails lane now names a gate that failed without printing a `FAIL` row. This branch's own `gate (guardrails)` job printed `guardrails: 1 failing` with nothing above it, and the failure took a second pass to attribute because the log never said which check it was. `drain` and the reporter the two dist gates print through both record the gate's command and exit code, and report `FAIL <cmd> (exit N, printed no FAIL line)` only when the gate printed nothing at all: a gate that printed its own traceback keeps it, because that tail is what `scripts/pr_autofix.py` hands a repair turn. The autofixer's hand-rolled environment save and restores are one `without_env` helper. (D551; Refs #1862) |
| 0.942.0 | 2026-11-06 | The merge queue's candidate push now runs its gate on the `queue` runner lane, a the runner VM runner that takes no other job, so a batch no longer waits behind pull request runs for a free `ubuntu-latest` slot. The candidate also saves its Rust build cache, since no main run reaches that runner's cache. Pull request runs keep `ubuntu-latest`, and an `external` PR still waits for `run-ci` on the `external` runner. The workflow lane check now reads a `runs-on` that picks its lane by branch, so the new lane is compared against review.yml's like the others. (Closes #2032) |
| 0.941.0 | 2026-11-06 | `read` and `edit` take at most 16 MiB of one file (`READ_FILE_CAP`); past it the call is refused with a row naming the size, the cap and the grep call that reaches the rest, and a text that large is never held in the snapshot store, so one huge view no longer evicts every other file's tags. A text file that quotes `%PDF-` is no longer mistaken for a PDF: the marker counts past the first byte only in bytes that are not UTF-8. `write` and `edit` replace a file through a sibling temp file renamed over its name, after checking the name still names the file the gate opened, so a reader or a crash never sees a short file and a symlink swapped in before the rename is refused; the file's mode is kept, and a hard-linked file is still written in place so its other names see the new bytes; when no sibling can be staged or renamed the write falls back to the in-place one and the result says so, on `write`, on `edit` and on a `grep` apply. A document keeps the converter's own ceiling instead: past the text cap the file is probed once and taken under that ceiling, so a PDF read without `pages` still converts, a page selection parses only those pages, and a PDF past that ceiling is refused with the `pages=1-50` read that reaches its text, while a whole-file format is told no read converts it. An edit whose header names a missing path is refused with the one same-named file this session read, as `[path#TAG]`, instead of being rebound and written without the permission rules seeing that path. A section that changes nothing is noted while the other sections land, a file a formatter stripped of trailing spaces still edits under its old tag, a stale `REM` or `MV` warns about the delete or move rather than a head/tail insert, a stale-tag refusal points at the header its last line names rather than a re-read, and a program's read lines stay anchorable across consecutive edits. (Closes #1511) |
| 0.940.0 | 2026-11-06 | The autofixer runs again. Its worker builds yi into its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, while the review helper only looked for `target/debug/yi` under the checkout, so every fix died with "No such file or directory" and was deferred. The helper now looks for and returns yi where cargo builds it. (Closes #2033) |
| 0.939.0 | 2026-11-05 | A spawn refused after its directory was made leaves nothing behind. The directory is created before the container placement, the tree fork and the build, and each of those can refuse; a guard removes it unless the child's record is listed, so the family names only children that ran, and a worktree child refused for a directory that is no repository leaves no `sub-*` in the parent's `children/`. `/land` names the cause git gave it. A merge git refuses for a file the lane has uncommitted changes to starts no merge at all, and `merge --abort` then answered `fatal: There is no merge to abort (MERGE_HEAD missing)` in place of git's own message; with no `MERGE_HEAD` the landing now reports git's refusal, naming the lane to resolve it in. A conflict list stays fenced and bounded at twenty files, and past that it carries its own row: `[20 of 21 conflicted files (CONFLICT_FILES_MAX 20); git diff --name-only --diff-filter=U in <lane>]`. The landing-failed steer is bounded at 2,000 characters with the same kind of row. A lease expires at every depth. The clock and the loop that runs expiry lived in the plan timer, which only a depth-0 session wired, so a grandchild below depth 0 whose lease was revoked had its grace served by nobody and its lane stayed orphaned. The due-time pump is now one type, `Due` in the plan timer's module: the plan timer drives it with its stuck and dispatch jobs, and `attach_runtime` spawns a second one at every depth that runs the host's lease expiry and ends once its host is dropped. `yi ask`, `yi rpc` and a solo TUI end their host before exiting: every live child is revoked and repossessed at once, the repossessions running side by side so each gets the whole shutdown grace, so its lane comes back rather than outliving the session that owns it, and the runtime still shuts down within that bound. The solo TUI and a headless drive end the host on the runtime that runs the children's turns, before it shuts down, since a turn dropped with its runtime never settles and its repossession would wait out the grace. The three exit call sites have no binary-level test: only `lanes::an_ending_host_repossesses_a_live_worktree_child_inside_its_grace` and `lanes::an_ending_host_repossesses_its_children_side_by_side`, which call the host's end directly, prove the repossession. (Closes #1571) |
| 0.938.0 | 2026-11-05 | `scripts/record_changes.py` keeps `docs/CHANGELOG.md` under the 512000-byte blob cap itself (Refs #1743). When recording would leave the changelog past the cap, or it already is past it, the recorder moves the oldest rows verbatim into new `docs/archive/changelog-<lo>-to-<hi>.md` files, each under the cap, keeps the newest rows that fit in three quarters of the cap, and adds a pointer line per new archive beside the existing ones. A log that archiving cannot bring under the cap is left alone and the existing refusal names it. This run archives rows 0.250.0 to 0.647.0 into two files, taking the changelog from 940951 to 383614 bytes, so the `docs/CHANGELOG.md` entry leaves the blob allowlist; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` is past the cap too and stays on it. `check_changes` now refuses an edit or delete of an archive file main already holds. |
| 0.937.0 | 2026-11-05 | A thinking model's turn now hands its reasoning back to the provider on the next request. The chat mapper keeps OpenRouter's `reasoning_details` array, joined per index as the stream sent it, on the turn's thinking block and echoes it byte for byte on the assistant message of the next turn, and keeps a Google `thought_signature` off a tool call's `extra_content` and returns it there, so a Gemini or DeepSeek tool loop stops 400ing on its second request. A Responses request for a level asks for `reasoning.summary: "auto"` beside its effort, so a summary reaches the transcript; a turn that asks for no thinking asks for no summary. A turn whose only reasoning was an encrypted detail still carries it, sealed after the block's own events. The thinking block caches the parsed reasoning field and details in memory beside its signature envelope, never on disk: the chat builder borrows that cached array on later requests of the live session, so it does not re-parse or clone prior turns' reasoning details, and a block loaded from a session file re-reads them from the envelope. The envelope is the one stored copy. (Closes #1525) |
| 0.936.0 | 2026-11-05 | Every cut inside a compaction view now says at the cut what it kept, what cap cut it and the call that reads the rest back. The brief section's demotion past `BRIEF_LINE_CAP` opens `[Earlier]` with one `[… N of M brief lines, the newest (BRIEF_LINE_CAP 120); the rest: id spans below, merged past EARLIER_CAP 24; read("history://self/since/0")]` row, and a demoted span carries the count of lines it stands for (`(#t0..#t4, 5 lines)`) instead of a bare id range. Past `EARLIER_CAP` the oldest spans merge into one line that keeps the sum of their counts rather than dropping the older ids without a word. The no-summary marker, which rides three times (the summary, the notice, and where the messages sat), used to say only how many messages it took; it now says `kept N of M (context_window K tokens)` and names `read("history://self/since/0")` as the call that restores them. A reclaimed tool result names the lever that cut it, `reclaim.keep_turns`, beside the request it cut at and the `read("history://self/<entry>")` that restores it, so the model can tell a cut of its own choosing from the runtime's. Nothing here changes what the ledger stores: a cut is a view over the append-only session. A span stored before counts existed carries no count: the cut row reads `N of M or more brief lines` and a merge writes the span without a count rather than a false total. The no-summary marker counts kept and total over the messages the view still holds, notes and the replaced summary aside. (Closes #1759) |
| 0.935.0 | 2026-11-05 | `annotate`'s and `get_context`'s descriptions were the two longest left in the tool table, and both were read by every turn whether or not the tool was called. Each is now a one-line summary naming `read("yi://tools/<name>")`, its full schema unchanged, and a `TOOL_GUIDES` row serves the text it used to carry whole. (D550; Closes #1565) |
| 0.934.0 | 2026-11-05 | The merge queue now builds each candidate from up to four of the oldest eligible PRs in enqueue order and lands a green batch with one non-force push. A red batch splits to its first half on current main instead of retrying whole, while a red singleton keeps its one retry before ejection. PRs that conflict only with an earlier batch member stay queued without a comment. The four fork-relative size checks announce a skip on the queue candidate because every member was judged at its own head. (Closes #1966) |
| 0.933.0 | 2026-11-05 | A forked child was seeded from the parent's stored history, so it re-read every tool result its parent's own requests had already replaced with a `[reclaimed: …]` placeholder: the bytes, not the view. `SubagentHost` now reads the parent through `AgentSession::history_handle`, which renders the parent's cuts the way its own requests send them, so a `fork=all` child starts from what its parent's model still sees; each placeholder names the parent and points at `history://parent/<entry>`, since `self` in the child is the child's own store. The seed's budget was the window less compaction's reserve, 92% of a 128,000-token window; it is now 80% of the child's own route window, never past its compaction threshold (which binds under an 81,920-token window), so the child's first request still has room for its brief and the turn it was forked to take, and the `[… fork kept K of N` row names that ceiling, the window it came from and `history://parent/since/0`. (Closes #1767) |
| 0.932.0 | 2026-11-05 | Seven open PRs carried `autofix:failed` and the comment beside it named nothing a person could act on (Refs #1873). Three of them were the fixer's own: a model turn that timed out was killed but its children were not, so a git it left running held the clone's `.git/index.lock` and lost the PR (#1779); a fix that changed nothing said only that and never what its model answered (#1709); and the failure label was set before its comment was posted, so a forge read that failed in between left a PR labelled with no reason at all (#1732, #1733). A timed-out turn now takes its whole process group with it. A fix that changed nothing quotes its model. The comment goes out before the label. A PR the pass cannot read is that PR's, so the rest of the pass still runs, and it counts toward the pass's quota because its fix may have paid. A conflict in a file the fixer may not touch now names `git fetch origin && git merge origin/<base>`, and the deferred line no longer blames the provider for a held lock or a full disk. (Closes #1873) |
| 0.931.0 | 2026-11-05 | Compaction no longer fires on every request when the kept tail is already over `compaction.at`. A compaction records the size of the messages it left (rebuilt from the latest compaction entry on resume or rewind), and the next ceiling compaction waits until the messages have grown past that by one `reserve`. The model window's own reserve line is not held back, so a request that would leave no room for the reply still compacts. The dogfood run that found this compacted 16 times in 48 requests and cost 66% more than the run with compaction off; the summarizer fires once now and again only when the history actually grew. (Closes #1757) |
| 0.930.0 | 2026-11-05 | The kernel holds less and does less per cell. It launches with IPython's output cache off (`--InteractiveShell.cache_size=0`), so a value shown as a cell's last expression is freed by `del`, and Yi's own snapshot and listing cells no longer store history, so `In` grows by one per model cell. The debounced snapshot after a clean cell re-dills only what the cells since the last pass could have changed: a name assigned, mutated through an attribute or subscript, or passed to a non-builtin call (and the globals a function named there reads), plus any name bound to the same object as one of those; every other variable whose id, type and size are unchanged keeps its last blob, so a change reached only through a nested reference or a callee's globals waits for a full pass. Every 20th cell attempts one, a cell that does not compile is skipped rather than forcing one, and the dispose flush (bounded at 5 s) is full whenever an incremental pass committed since the last full one. A read-only `print(len(x))` over 60,000 grep-hit dicts took 145, 170 and 20 ms against 8.6 to 35.8 s for a full pass in the kernel venv. Compaction no longer deletes a variable over the 16 MiB per-variable cap, which was its only copy: it stays live and unsaved, and `KernelManager::prune_oversized_variables` is gone, along with the `Out` chase it needed. The bootstrap's names (`fetch`, `bash`, `yi` and the four Python skills) join the snapshot's and the listing's skip set, so a fresh kernel's compaction note says it has no names yet, and that note lists at most forty names with a `[… 40 of N names (NAMES_SHOWN 40); the rest: %who]` row. `python/yi_runtime/uv.lock`, which nothing in the unpacked runtime reads, is left out of the embedded archive, cutting the deflated Python stream from 90,705 to 59,536 bytes. The plugin-installer code (`pinned`, `tarball`, `uv_install`) moves under `crates/kernel/src/install/` and `civil_from_days` to `date.rs`, re-exported at the old paths. The builder-path fallback in `python_root` the issue names was already gone on main (it falls back to `/nonexistent/yi/python`). ledger+: dill snapshot \| a pass after a cell re-dills only what the cells since the last pass could have changed (an assigned name, one mutated through an attribute or subscript, one passed to a non-builtin call, and any name bound to the same object), every 20th cell attempts a full pass and the dispose flush is full after any incremental one (`kernel_e2e::a_read_only_cell_leaves_untouched_variables_undilled`, `kernel_e2e::a_mutation_through_an_alias_survives_a_restart`, `kernel_e2e::dispose_heals_a_nested_mutation_no_cell_named`); a variable over 16 MiB stays live and unsaved through compaction, and the post-compaction note lists only the model's names, at most forty with a `%who` row (`snapshot_e2e::post_compaction_sync_reports_only_the_models_names`); the kernel keeps no `Out` cache and Yi's own cells stay out of `In` (`kernel_e2e::a_displayed_value_is_freed_by_del`) (Closes #1578) |
| 0.929.0 | 2026-11-05 | A successful Python `ipython` result longer than 10,000 characters now ends with the next line `open(path,'w').write(var)` and what it does: it writes a kernel variable to a file without printing it or re-typing it through `write`. Session 01a04c94 printed 26,490 and then 18,747 characters of a variable it later wrote out, and re-typed the second through `write` for 6,725 output tokens, about a fifth of the parent's non-cached tokens, although the orchestrate prose already said to compute in program space. The line rides the procedural graph (`large_print` fact, one `ipython` to `write` edge in `graph.json`), asserted in `facts_of` beside the coroutine facts, so it lands on the print before the expensive write and needs no Python change, hence no runtime identity hash move. The threshold is the `LARGE_PRINT_CHARS` constant, not an environment variable. Shell cells and failed cells are left out: no variable exists there. The row was filed as unproven: if a repeat run does not move, delete the edge and the fact. (Refs #79) |
| 0.928.0 | 2026-11-05 | The autofix sweep now runs after every push to main as well as on its schedule. Before any agent work on a behind PR, it tries a plain merge with main's baseline driver. A clean merge is pushed through the existing trusted path without a model call; a real conflict keeps the existing fixer path, whose brief names the conflicted files. `autofix:hold` still keeps the bot out, and a PR updated in the last five minutes is left alone so the bot does not race active work. (Closes #2015) |
| 0.927.0 | 2026-11-05 | The planted gitleaks fixtures are fake keys the scanners have to catch, and GitHub's push protection catches them too, so the mirror's push was refused with `GH013` and the public mirror stopped publishing. `scripts/mirror/exclude.txt` now drops `scripts/scan/fixtures/gitleaks/`, and `check_public_surface.py --selfcheck` asserts that every planted fixture in that directory is excluded, so a line removed again fails the gate instead of the next push. Semgrep kept a per-file time limit, at 300 seconds instead of 30: a loaded runner exceeded 30 on one rule in `scripts/pr_autofix.py`, and `--timeout 0` answered that by removing the limit for every file the guardrails scan. A `Timeout` entry in semgrep's report is now announced as a `skip semgrep <path> (<rule> ran over 300s)` row naming the file and the rule it gave up on, and every other error in the report still fails the scan. `check_scan.py --selfcheck` judges the argv's `--timeout` and both directions of that split, and its `semgrep-timeout` mutant is refused like the others. The autofix pass drops its worker to a throwaway user, and on the guardrails runner the interpreter was a shim under a root-only `HOME`: the job died on `PermissionError: [Errno 13] Permission denied: '/root/.local/bin/python3.12'` before the worker started. The box now carries a readable copy of the interpreter and runs the worker from it, and the selfcheck proves both halves of that choice: a reachable interpreter runs where it is, and a root-only one is carried in and left readable to the user it drops to. Two edges of that carry are now held. An interpreter unreadable to the dropped-to user, and outside the prefix the box can copy, is refused with a message naming it instead of being handed to the worker, because a path that user cannot exec only moves the `PermissionError` one call deeper. And widening the copied prefix's modes skips every symlink the copy carries, so no target outside the box is chmod'd: before, a link in the runner's prefix to a root-only file made the fixer widen that file in place. `check_scan.py --selfcheck` also drives `semgrep()` itself now, over a faked report, so the announced skip row is judged where the model and the CI log read it rather than one call short of the print. The selfcheck's stand-in cargo runs under the interpreter the worker itself uses, so a root-only runner interpreter cannot make the fixture unexecutable to the user it drops to, and a worker that leaves no reach report now fails the selfcheck by name instead of a traceback naming the file. The lookup of that interpreter is guarded where the fixture is built, and a refusal is a hand-off rather than the PR's failure: before, a runner whose box cannot carry its interpreter died with a traceback out of the selfcheck, and the pass that reached it was written into the PR's ledger as a failed attempt with a retry spent. (Refs #1733) |
| 0.926.0 | 2026-11-05 | `just pr merge` no longer updates a pull request merely because main moved. A behind, conflict-free PR merges as it stands, or goes to the queue when the merge queue is on; a real conflict still takes the explicit update path. A queue landing now runs the recorder on its candidate and pushes the candidate and record together, with the record authored by yi-queue so postmerge does not record it again. (Closes #2007) |
| 0.925.0 | 2026-11-05 | A compaction summary that quotes a user message by address (`user://3: "…"`) is now checked against that message. The summarizer's quote is compared, whitespace aside, with the typed message the address names; a quote that message does not contain, or an address no typed message has, gets ` [unverified: not in user://N]` after it in the summary the model reads. Before, nothing verified the quotes `prompts.rs` asks for, so a tool result carrying a planted "user said: …" line could come back after compaction as the user's own goal. A quote copied from the key row's escaped form (`\n`, `\"`) or wrapped onto the next line is checked too. The marker is part of the summary the compaction entry stores; the cited user messages are unchanged, and a summary produced without the session's user index (no store) is left as written. (Closes #1761) |
| 0.924.0 | 2026-11-05 | Every test that spawns the `yi` binary now spawns it through one harness, `crates/cli/tests/support/yi_bin.rs`, which pins `HOME` to the test's own scratch dir, and `scripts/guardrails/check_test_home.py` refuses a test file that names `CARGO_BIN_EXE_yi` anywhere else, or takes the bare path from `yi_bin::bin()` outside a short allowlist, so a new spawn cannot forget. The issue records 11,633 of the 11,635 checkpoint dirs under the owner's real `~/.yi` (2.8 GB) on 2026-10-07 belonging to test temp dirs that no longer existed — `yi-rpc-*` 2,703, `yi-*` 1,942, `yi-acp-*` 1,883 — because a spawn that left `HOME` alone wrote the developer's home. The gate also proves its own refutation: `--selfcheck` builds a tree with a stray reference beside the harness and requires the stray to be the one refused, and a harness that drops the `HOME` pin turns the new test red with the developer's home in the failure line. Tests that must prove the binary's refusal of an unset `HOME` keep that ability through `yi_command_homeless`, named so the exception is visible. (Closes #1123) |
| 0.923.0 | 2026-11-05 | # Stop a parent kernel unpickling what a contained child wrote D240 gave every child's kernel the family board as a writable root, so `rlm.put` in a child reached `rlm.get` in its parent. The same grant is write access to the directory the parent's kernel calls `dill.load` on: a walled child — the member D240 was protecting — could dill a `__reduce__` to the board, and the root's unconfined kernel ran it on the next `rlm.fetch("kernel://<child>/<var>")`. `Wall::contained` now answers the question both ends ask: any wall, a container, or a checkout of its own (`isolated`, set from the spawn's isolation and inherited through `Wall::under`, which also sets it under a container parent, since the container itself is never handed down). `session_sandbox` names the board in that member's `deny_write`, which outranks the writable root `kernel_profile` grants: the child's kernel meets `EPERM` on `rlm.put`, and so does its `bash()`. Reading up still works, as text. `Resolver::dump_kernel` asks the member's own kernel whether it may write the board (`KernelService::writes_board`, the same deny read back), and answers `None` when it may not; the idling host handler falls through to the ordinary text read, which `rlm.fetch` already returns when a reply carries no path. So a parent can still read what a contained child computed, and never loads its pickle. The `fetch` host handler moved from `wiring.rs` to `fetch::serve`: `wiring.rs` is past the 1,000-line ceiling and may not grow, and the handler was the fetch layer's own. (D549; Closes #1538) |
| 0.922.0 | 2026-11-04 | `yi update` no longer installs a release older than the yi it replaces. It says `already 0.898.0, newer than the latest release 0.799.0; yi update --downgrade installs it` and leaves the binary alone; `--downgrade` installs the older one. It also runs the downloaded binary's `--version` before replacing anything, so a release that cannot start on this machine, which nothing checks for a Mac binary cross-linked on Linux, is refused and the old yi stays. A release now goes out every night. `.forgejo/workflows/release.yml` builds main's version for Apple-silicon Macs and for static x86_64 Linux on the forge's runner, signs both with the release key, and publishes them on the forge. It then builds the same version from the public mirror's tree and publishes it on GitHub, where a public build's `yi update` now looks. Each release stays a draft until every archive is uploaded, so a night that dies part way leaves nothing `yi update` reads, and the next night deletes the draft and starts again. The GitHub half builds only the mirror commit the forge release became, checked against filter-repo's commit map. Forge releases past the newest seven are deleted. The release key is new. The old key's passphrase was lost, so it could sign nothing, here or in CI; `allowed_signers`, `scripts/install.sh` and the README now trust `SHA256:Tb4DlV9Kzm6hIEEnTNTjGNPv4msLyf4zlFPIZZlpsOE`. A yi built before this change trusts only the old key, so its `yi update` refuses the first release signed with the new one; reinstall it once with `scripts/install.sh`. (D548; Closes #2023) |
| 0.921.0 | 2026-11-04 | A compaction view now compiles from everything the cut summarizes away, the prefix of a split turn included. One prompt followed by many tool turns used to compile an empty view, because the view stopped at the turn's start and the whole turn is the split prefix. An `ipython` call is briefed as `ipython <first non-blank code line>` instead of the bare tool name. A bash command in a brief is cut at `BRIEF_LINE_CHARS` with a `[… K of T chars (BRIEF_LINE_CHARS 160); the rest: read history://self]` row, as are the other brief fields. The read and modified file lists in a summary stop at `FILE_LIST_CAP` (100) files and end in a row naming kept of total, the cap and the `history://self` grep that finds the rest. The two summarization prompts nothing called (`SUMMARIZATION_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, `TURN_PREFIX_SUMMARIZATION_PROMPT`) are deleted. The rescue after a failed summary charges its room with an empty view, so the newest whole tool steps stay, and the view rides in only what the kept tail leaves spare. (Closes #1506) |
| 0.920.0 | 2026-11-04 | `yi gc` and a lane's release now recognise a squash-merged branch. They ask, cheapest first: the same commit, an ancestor, an empty three-dot diff, the same tree, the branch's changed paths agreeing with trunk's, and matching patch-ids. The path list is read NUL-separated and every path goes back as a literal pathspec, so a name git would quote is compared rather than read as no change at all. A report line says which rung: `removed <path> (merged: same content)`. Dirty and locked trees stay as before, and a question git could not answer keeps the branch: a release names that reason instead of calling it unmerged, and an orphaned slot asks the same question. Past 256 commits on either side of the fork the patch-id walk stops and says so; a changed-path list past 1024 skips that rung and lets the walk answer, and speaks only when the walk cannot answer either. A dry-run `yi gc` writes nothing: no rung merges or stores an object to answer. A path that is not UTF-8, or a merge commit with content of its own, keeps the branch. (D547; Closes #1942) |
| 0.919.0 | 2026-11-03 | A stop is judged by a check the harness runs, not by the model's prose. The `spec` tool (`freeze`, `run`, `add`, `view`) holds requirements quoted from the request (`R1`..`Rn`), the artifact paths the task names and one check whose stdout is TAP lines naming requirement ids; `freeze` runs that check on the untouched tree and types every requirement `f2p` (red there, must turn green) or `p2p` (green there, must stay green), refusing with every failing fact at once: an `f2p` already green at the baseline, a `p2p` red there, an id the check never prints, no `f2p`, an artifact that is the cwd or `/`, a check that prints no TAP, a metric without its line, and fewer requirements than the request enumerates. A run is the harness's own: a `bash` call naming the checker is not a run, so it never clears a red id. The check is a command the model wrote, so it runs the way `bash` runs one: keyless (no provider key in its environment) and inside the session sandbox, on the baseline, on every run and on both refute mutants. The stop rule refuses a clean stop of a request that enumerated items when no spec was frozen, when the last run is older than the last write (which every writer tool's own result records, so a fresh green run cannot outlive an edit), when any requirement is red (naming each id with the check's first diagnostic line) and when the refute — the baseline plus the empty and restored artifacts — shows the check cannot tell the fixed tree from the untouched one. The refusal repeats; with `--deadline` the clock is the only other exit, and without one the same refusal waives after three and records that it did. Todo and plan steps do not yet bind to requirement ids, and the prompt doctrine is not rewritten: both are the plan's later stages and land under their own issues. The environment block carries `spec: R1..Rn green\|red, run <age>`, or says the run is stale; the skill at `skills/yi/spec/` teaches the freeze-run-refute loop. (D546; Closes #334) |
| 0.918.0 | 2026-11-03 | `memory.search`, `yi memory search` and `compact.search` now read a non-ASCII letter as part of its word. Before, `résumé` was indexed as `r` and `sum`, so an accented query returned notes and messages that only shared a fragment. Tokens are runs of Unicode letters, digits and `_`, lowercased with full Unicode case folding. `.`, `/` and `-` still join paths and identifiers, and the 61 English function words still drop. A history hit's snippet still centres on the first query word under the same lowercase fold. `history.search` now builds its BM25 index once for each state of the session files and reuses it until a file's length, mtime or inode changes, where before it rebuilt the index on every query. A cold session file is parsed with the lock held only for that file, so other searches no longer wait on it. (D545; Closes #1547) |
| 0.917.0 | 2026-11-03 | `just commit <subject> [<path>...]` stages the paths it is given by name instead of running `git add -A`, the one command `.ruler/090-workflow.md` forbids in a shared tree. With no path it commits what is already staged; with nothing staged it refuses and prints the dirty paths so the caller names them. The ratchet commit it makes first still takes only the baselines. With paths named, the commit itself is limited to them, so a file another session staged stays staged. `forge_pr.py --selfcheck` requires both: an unnamed untracked file stays out, and an unrelated staged file is not committed with the named one. The autofixer no longer builds yi or refreshes the OpenRouter catalog on every 15-minute pass: `.forgejo/workflows/autofix.yml` drops both steps and `pr_review.yi_bin` builds and refreshes once at the first model call (its own selfcheck pins that). The workflow half is a step deletion with no test of its own (Refs #1310). (Closes #1512) |
| 0.916.0 | 2026-11-03 | The three adapters no longer copy a request's history through `transform_messages` and `json!` on the way to the wire, and a streamed tool call's arguments are parsed once instead of on every delta (Refs #1558). `transform_messages` returns borrowed messages and copies only one it rewrites, and the adapters move their message and image values into the body.
