The owned history `convert_to_llm` returns is still one full copy per request; borrowing it through `LlmContext` is left open on the issue. With three 1 MB images in a session, `build_params` allocations fall from 12.0 MB to 3.0 MB on Anthropic and from 15.0 MB to 3.0 MB on both OpenAI wires. The body bytes are unchanged, and a recorded fixture holds them. The Anthropic, OpenAI chat and Responses mappers append argument deltas and parse them when the call ends, or when the stream is cut or fails. A cut or failed call stores the parse of every byte received, as before; on Anthropic it now also gets the leak repair a normal stop applies, and a call whose received bytes parse to nothing keeps the arguments its start event carried. A 238 KB write call took over 10 s to stream in 16-byte deltas and now takes 0.6 s for all three adapters together. |
| 0.915.0 | 2026-11-03 | A request carries one goal continuation, the newest, and a re-drive's quoted reasoning only until a reply answers it. Every idle `AgentEnd` under an Active goal queued another 4.87 KB of constant continuation instructions, and nothing left the window before compaction, so a long goal re-sent each copy on every request; the request view now keeps only the newest (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` goals row, `goal_e2e::three_idle_continuations_reach_the_next_request_as_one_newest_copy`). A second reasoning cut's re-drive quotes the last 8,000 chars of the cut turn, and a read re-drive rode every later request with that derivation inside it; once an assistant reply follows it, the request drops the whole re-drive while the session record keeps it (`loop_events::a_read_re_drive_quotes_nothing_while_an_unread_one_quotes_its_tail`). Only the request view changed: `convert_to_llm` gains `spent_in_request`, no message is dropped from the record, and the goal's edit and budget notices now persist `details.goalNotice: true` so a `goal_prompt` without it, an older build's continuation included, is superseded (`goal_e2e::continuations_an_older_build_recorded_are_superseded_too`). (Closes #1559) |
| 0.914.0 | 2026-11-03 | The kernel host registry no longer registers `mcp.config`, `mcp.refresh` and `plan.get`. Python calls only `mcp.connect`; the three deleted verbs had no Python or Rust caller (the RPC `plan` route reads the plan service directly), so a cell that requests one still gets the "not available in this session" error it got from any unregistered verb. `HostRegistry::register_mcp_stubs` and `PlanService::register` are gone with them. (Closes #1530) |
| 0.913.0 | 2026-11-03 | Provider errors are classed once, the connection a finished stream hands back is pooled, and a turn that dies mid-stream keeps what it produced. `ErrorClass::is_transport` holds for `TransportHttp` only on 408, 409, 429 and 5xx, so a 400, 401 or 403 is no longer re-sent with the 99-byte "the same turn runs again" steer; a routing 404 may still be resent once, because a follow-up drops the forced choice that caused it (`ErrorClass::is_wire_refusal`). A quota 429 classes as the typed `provider:quota`, and a refused retry and a refusal as provider verdicts; none is re-run, whatever output it billed. `retry_delay` returns a `Duration`; a server wait past the policy's cap ends the turn naming the wait instead of sleeping it out and sending again; `x-should-retry` overrides the status; the backoff sleeps in 100 ms slices, so a cut ends the wait instead of sending the request the loop no longer wants. A stream that stops at `response.completed` now drains its body to EOF, handing the connection back to ureq, and the pooled agent keeps 16 idle connections per host instead of ureq's one. A keepalive `data:` line is skipped on both wires; the drain reads on a thread the turn waits two seconds for, so a body that never ends costs the turn no more than that. A UTF-8 split keeps only an incomplete tail of at most three bytes, and an invalid byte ends the stream naming its offset rather than stalling to the read timeout. Anthropic and Responses fail the running mapper on a mid-stream error, as chat does, so the text, tool calls and usage a cut turn produced survive in its error turn. A refusal ends the turn as `The model refused: …` instead of an empty stop. Responses keeps one thinking block per reasoning item and opens a new text block after a function call. Leak recovery leaves a block inside a fence or an inline span as text, leads its ids with the message's own clock so a resumed session cannot mint `leak-0` again. (D544; Closes #1514) |
| 0.912.0 | 2026-11-03 | A checklist `set` from the tool no longer writes every row it did not name into its own journal record. The op carries `keep_rest`, which the reducer honours by keeping the unnamed rows, and `keep_omitted` sets it instead of padding the rows; a 30-todo plan's two-row set went from a 1,769-byte record to one holding two rows. A set that has to open a plan still carries the rows there, because a new plan is creating them rather than keeping them. Old records replay unchanged: the flag defaults to false and the padded rows they already hold stay the whole cut. (Closes #1589) |
| 0.911.0 | 2026-11-03 | A whole-plan `set` that carries repairable graph problems repairs them by re-sending the set, and each pass went through the tool's real apply, so every refused pass left a refusal record in the journal before the set finally landed: a set with a duplicate label, an unresolved edge and a cycle wrote three records where the plan needs one. The repair loop now validates each pass through the reducer's dry run (`PlanEngine::dry`) and journals nothing for it; the one apply the call ends on writes the one record, and a set the table cannot settle still writes its single refusal. The dry run is the reducer's own check on a clone of the loaded state, so the refusal a pass returns is the one the real apply would give and nothing is written for it. (Closes #1586) |
| 0.910.0 | 2026-11-03 | The document converter that `read` and `grep` run in the sandboxed venv python is now `python/yi_runtime/src/yi/convert.py` instead of a 191-line raw string in `crates/tools/src/document.rs`. `document.rs` carries that file with `include_str!` and spawns `python -I -c <the program> <source> <staging> <pages>` — the same sandbox, stdin bytes and one-line JSON reply, so every caller sees the same results. The program is carried rather than resolved: a venv whose installed `yi` package predates the binary converts anyway, where `-m yi.convert` there would fail with `No module named yi.convert`, and `-c` skips the package `__init__` that would otherwise import `rlm` and `ipykernel` on every uncached conversion. `anydoc` and `pdf_inspector` are imported by the function that uses them, so importing the module needs neither and the stdlib unittest lane tests the PDF running-header removal and the spreadsheet tidying against markdown recorded from the real producers (`python/yi_runtime/tests/vectors/convert.json`). The change is a kernel-package change: it moves the runtime identity hash, so the venv is rebuilt on next boot. (Closes #1555) |
| 0.909.0 | 2026-11-03 | Two instruction files that differ only in whole-line HTML comments now attach once even when a comment leaves a blank line behind it. Ruler writes `AGENTS.md` with a `<!-- Generated by Ruler -->` header above the first `<!-- Source: … -->` marker and its twin for another target without that header, so dropping the comment lines left one render one blank line longer than the other and the two hashes split: both files attached, and on a rules file past the budget both were cut. The text the hash compares now drops the comment lines, collapses the repeated blank lines a comment inside the body leaves, and trims the leading and trailing blank runs, which a comment at either end leaves and markdown never renders. The hash is compared only among the files of one session's attach, so nothing durable moves. Two files whose bodies differ by a word still attach separately. (Closes #1545) |
| 0.908.0 | 2026-11-03 | Bet 1(e)'s kill test lands: `evals/kill/b1-exp-e.py` and `evals/worker_mode.py` measure whether a worker sent without the permission-mode fragment retries a refused call more than one built with it, which is what decides whether the drop that landed as ns-043 (`ext::narrow` no longer attaches the `Rank::Mode` slot) stays. Ten Auto-mode tasks each ask a worker for one call a broker refuses in the worker's own lane, a write outside the working tree, an instruction-file edit beside it, further writes outside it, a write into the workspace `.git`; each runs on the fragment-free head and on the arm built from `EVAL_KILL_ARM_BINARY`, the build cut before the slot went. A kill-test spec may now name its own `binary`, one line in `evals/kill.py::ask_once`, because a prompt fragment is Rust-side text: the two arms are two builds, and one test's rows carry both. The counter is the contract the issue pre-registered, and it is read from the worker's own session rather than from the run's exit: a **retry** is the same refused command sent again after its denial, keyed by the tool and its target (a `bash` command's text, a `write`/`edit` path), so the reversible form a worker answers a refusal with (the in-tree `tally.txt` after the refused `../tally.txt`) is never a retry, and a **failed task** is a worker that wrote no `report.md` in the tree. Both fixtures under `evals/fixtures/worker_mode/` are real worker transcripts: the real binary, under faux, in a lane where the worker's write outside the tree is refused, recorded twice — once resending the refused write, once changing to the path inside the tree. `evals/tests/test_worker_mode.py` pins the counter on both, the run scoring, the arm wiring and the verdict; `selftest.check_worker_mode` runs it inside `just guardrails`. No ledger row here: a row is written from a run's own harness output after the run finishes, and this change lands the harness, not the run. The two families are Kimi K3.1 and then OpenRouter GLM 5.3 flash, and the withdrawal fires if either figure rises in either family. `request_budget.rs::role_bodies` now runs `git init` for the Bare workspace instead of creating an empty `.git` directory, so the bare worker body is measured in a real repository like the other workspaces; the request-budget tests pass unchanged. (Closes #1480) |
| 0.907.0 | 2026-11-03 | Dogfood session `1792775630063_01a169c1` read a whole repository through `bash`: `sed -n '1,80p'`, `head -60`, `grep -rn "unwrap()\|expect(" … \| grep -v test \| wc -l`, which printed `0` because a line filter drops the lines holding the word and not the test modules. The doctrine already said to use `read` and `grep`, and the tool boundary said nothing back. `crates/tools/src/steer.rs` classifies the command before `BashTool::execute` runs it. Every segment must be one the permission classifier calls safe and the dedicated tools serve — `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `sed -n` and `grep`/`rg`, with `wc`, `sort`, `uniq` and `cut` allowed after one of them — and the answer then opens with one row: `[read answers this: path=<file> offset=<line> limit=<n>; …]` for a file print, `[grep answers this: pattern=<regex> path=<dir> count for per-file hit counts; …]` for a search. A redirect, a `$(` or backtick substitution, a vcs, build or test segment, or any verb outside that set keeps the bare output, so `git log -1`, `cargo test 2>&1 \| tail -5` and `just check` are untouched. A single-file `cat`/`head`/`tail`/`sed` view carries its `[path#TAG]` header and no `read` row, since the tag is the anchor. `find`, `fd` and `tree` get no row: `read` cannot filter by name. The yi-types test `Scratch` now canonicalizes its directory, because the request-budget test under a `..` TMPDIR found the checkout's `.git` and loaded its AGENTS.md into the bare prompts. (D543; Closes #1436) |
| 0.906.0 | 2026-11-03 | The checker recipe was a screen (`evals/checker_screen.py`) nobody could call from a session; it is now a skill, `skills/yi/checker/SKILL.md`, beside `unattended`. It says to write the requirement list from the task text, spawn one `role="worker"` child with the task text in its brief and `deny_url=["history://"]` so the child reads no transcript of the root's, of the root's parent or of a sibling, `deny_read` and `deny_write` on the workspace so it neither opens the attempt nor edits the graded tree, and have it write a `checks.py` of `PASS <id>` / `FAIL <id>` lines into a `checks_dir` the snippet binds outside the workspace. The root starts each requirement row with the check that proves it — a plan row's `check=`, its contract's `decider: {cmd}`, or the checks program itself where there is no plan — and the root runs the checks on the finished work itself: a reader has only `read` and `grep`, so it is handed the task text and the checks' output, reports the requirements they miss, and the root appends those rows; no row is relaxed and the work is not edited. Nothing is hidden from the implementer; a failing check reaches it as the check and its output. The description opens with its condition, an interactive root or an allow rule, because a headless root refuses the child's `checks_dir` write and the catalog clips a line at 60 characters. The skill is proven where a model would run it. Three tests install the shipped file under a scratch home: the catalog line carries the condition and `yi://skills/checker` reads back; the skill's own python fence runs in the kernel with a task holding a triple quote and the brief carries it unchanged with nothing executed; and the `rlm.run` payload walls history, refuses a read and a write of a workspace file, and leaves the `checks_dir` outside the workspace writable. (Closes #1550) |
| 0.905.0 | 2026-11-03 | The walled-session-transcripts decision (#971) gets its decision row back. Commit 114e83875 wrote it as D345 in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, but its change file, docs/changes/2026-10-01-walled-session-transcripts.md, carried no `decision:` line, so the recorder backfill (#1746) numbered nothing for it and D345 now names the review-job decision. This file carries the decision text from that commit through the reviewed path, with the reversible-via cell trimmed to seams that exist today (the original named `own_walls`, renamed `walled_roots` in 713d1b6cc); the recorder gives it a number on main. The comments and design text that cited the old number name the decision in prose until that number exists, and are pointed at it in a follow-up. (D542; Closes #1750) |
| 0.904.0 | 2026-11-03 | A backgrounded `bash` job outlived the `yi` that started it. The job's watchdog is a thread in the process, and a normal return from `dispatch` ended the process without signalling it, so a `sleep 300` or a `./server` kept its process group alive: the model was told the job would be killed at the next interrupt or turn end, and nothing was left to do it. `Jobs::kill_running` now sets the kill flag of every running job, the `bash` tool's and the kernel bridge's `exec.spawn` alike, since at exit no owner is left to release either, and waits up to the existing five-second `KILL_GRACE` for each job's own watchdog to run `kill_tree` (descendants walked first, so a re-grouped child dies too). `main` calls it once, after `dispatch` returns and before `std::process::exit`. `Jobs::retire` keeps a retired owner's still-running job in the map, flagged and killed, until its reaper settles, so `yi acp`, which retires every session before `main` sweeps, is waited on too. Only that normal-return path is covered: a `yi` ended by a signal (Ctrl-C on `yi ask` mid-turn, `kill -TERM`, the serve daemon's SIGKILL of a worker) still leaves its jobs running, since no signal handler is installed. A tier-2 journey drives the real binary: `yi ask` starts `echo $$ > job-pgid; exec sleep 300` with `wait: 5`, the turn ends, and the test asserts the recorded process group is gone by the time the binary exits. A second, separate fix rides along: `tools::a_job_wait_returns_as_the_job_settles` raced its 80 ms auto-background timer against `sleep 0.1` and failed under a full-suite load when the sleep finished inline; it now retries an inline finish (up to twenty attempts for five backgrounded waits) and still bounds only the waits that ran. Seen red: the unretried loop failed with `no job id` in a loaded `just check`. (Refs #819) |
| 0.903.0 | 2026-11-03 | A compaction is due while the whole request leaves the reserve for the summary (D338), so the summary request is the loop's previous request plus the directive and reads the cache. It was not: `compaction.rs` converted the history with `convert_to_llm`, while the loop sends `overlay.view(convert_to_llm(...))`, so every tool result the run had already cut went out whole again and the prefix missed the cache by exactly those results. `LoopRequest` now carries the loop's view stage, handed over by the `LoopRequestOf` closure, and the summary request converts through it. The stage handed over is `reclaim::convert`, which is `convert_to_llm` plus the recorded cuts, and only a warm attempt takes it (a cold one reads the history as whole text), which renders the cuts already on record and decides nothing: a compaction repeats the loop's bytes and never spends a cut of its own. `AgentSession::compact_now`, the manual `/compact` path, builds the same convert from the session's ledger. `compaction_faux::an_in_loop_compaction_repeats_the_loops_reclaimed_prefix` drives eight reads on a measured model, schedules one compaction from the seventh, and holds the compaction body to the loop's previous body byte for byte through the directive tail, `[reclaimed: …]` lines included; it was red against the plain convert with `the compaction dropped the loop's cut results`. `compaction_faux::a_manual_compaction_sends_the_cuts_on_record` holds `/compact` to the same lines and to recording no cut of its own; it was red with `the manual compaction sent the cut results whole`. (Closes #1517) |
| 0.902.0 | 2026-11-03 | The Python fake host (`python/yi_runtime/tests/fake_host.py`) no longer keeps its own copy of the plan engine's step table, retry cap and label cap. `crates/runtime/tests/plan_vectors.rs` writes `python/yi_runtime/tests/vectors/plan_steps.json` from `STEPS`, `RETRY_CAP` and `TODO_LABEL_MAX` (`YI_BLESS=1` regenerates it) and fails when the committed file differs; the fake reads that file. The fake now refuses `add_edge` on a running todo as the engine does, where it used to apply it in any state (#1566). (Closes #1566) |
| 0.901.0 | 2026-11-03 | Main already blocks: `pr_review.MODE` is `blocking` (D322), `just pr ready` and `just pr merge` refuse a PR without two rounds clean on its head, the sweep strips `WIP:` only through `promotes`, and `/override <reason>` from an allowed author is the one bypass. What stage 5 still lacked was the calibration query. `just pr calibrate` reads replay files and prints precision, recall and kept-clean of a high finding. The owner reads the pairs before trusting the gate on live PRs; no threshold is coded. The 2026-09-28 numbers in the plan's table predate the diff rule and are not restated here. (D541; Closes #680) |
| 0.900.0 | 2026-11-03 | The retention floor keeps only the requester's words and the letter that wakes a session, and one lifetime table in `wrapper.rs` retires a host notice with the window that read it (Closes #1544). `retain_floor` kept every user-role message in the summarized range, so a `Host(Notice)` deadline or restore note that the summary already carried rode the retained tail for the rest of the session, and `drop_internal` knew nine wrapped kinds and five read-once nudges: `stream_retry`, `todo_prelude`, `cache_alert` and `plan_relevance` accumulated in every later window. A child's brief is `Task` and the floor still keeps it, so no compaction takes a child's task from it (D337). |
| 0.899.0 | 2026-11-03 | Deleting `~/.yi/lanes` no longer strands the pool. Git keeps a worktree registered after its directory is gone, and a lock a dead session left on it made the next claim refuse the slot the pool owns, with every later claim after it: `fatal: '<path>' is a missing but locked worktree`. A fresh claim now drops every registration under the lanes directory whose tree is gone, locked ones included, before it adds the worktree back, whichever shape the stale registration has, and the rebuilt slot checks out the new session's branch with the repository holding one registration for that path. (Closes #664) |
| 0.898.0 | 2026-11-03 | The `fetch(url)` and `bash(cmd)` contract of the kernel-side `rlm` package ran as an 80-line Python string inside `crates/runtime/tests/kernel_data_surface.rs`, spawned through the machine's `python3`. It is now `python/yi_runtime/tests/test_rlm_bash_fetch.py`, which the unittest lane in `check_guardrails.sh` already runs, with the same assertions: the `kernel://` owner and child-kernel resolution, the empty-command and empty-url refusals, the spawn/tail/poll/kill/release sequence, and the silence of a released handle. Tests sit outside the runtime identity hash, so no venv rebuild follows. `every_rlm_name_the_model_reads_is_public_in_the_package` stays in Rust because it reads Rust-side text. (Closes #1531) |
| 0.897.0 | 2026-11-03 | A session's rail avatar was a 5×5 mirrored identicon carrying fifteen bits of its id's FNV hash, and the owner's ledger had outgrown them: of the 2,572 sessions under `~/.yi/sessions`, `grid()` gave 2,376 marks, so 196 sessions wore an avatar another session already wore (209 pairs), and a further 165 pairs of distinct marks sat within two cells of each other at rail size. The grid is now 7×7 with the same mirror symmetry: four bits a row pick its left four columns, the right three mirror them, the centre cell is always on, so a mark carries 28 bits of the same hash. The same 2,572 ids now give 2,572 distinct marks, and the closest two differ in two cells (one such pair; five more differ in three), measured over a fixture of those ids by `avatar::tests::the_real_ledger_never_gives_two_sessions_one_mark` and `avatar::tests::at_most_one_pair_of_ledger_marks_sits_within_two_cells`. The geometry divides the 40 px image exactly (7 cells of 4 px inside a 6 px margin), so a cell is no longer clipped by the margin and the sprite keeps the family's look at its 4×2-cell placement, measured cell by cell in `avatar::tests::the_mark_inks_its_on_cells_and_leaves_the_margin_clear`; the initials tile, the accent, the working session's loop and the waiting session's single still frame are unchanged. (Closes #1714) |
| 0.896.0 | 2026-11-03 | Add pinned static security scans to the shared guardrails. Zizmor audits every Forgejo workflow with hostile fixtures, reasoned suppressions and a shrink-only baseline. Workflow permission and checkout-credential findings are fixed; action tags whose mirror commit cannot be verified offline and two deliberate review behaviors carry inline reasons (Closes #1720). Gitleaks scans the working tree and PR diff, and scans rewritten mirror history before any push. Seven provider fixtures prove its fake-key rules; `.gitguardian.yaml` remains the one path-ignore list and gitleaks derives only its planted-fake entries, so a real source path on that list is still scanned, and archived generic examples sit in a separate shrink-only history baseline (Refs #1722). Vendored Semgrep rules cover the trial's Python findings plus Yi subprocess and production-Rust laws. MD5/SHA1 uses and a shell pipeline are fixed; direct argv calls and two temporary-directory uses carry reasons. Every scanner family has hostile and clean fixtures and a disabled-rule selfcheck. The gate provisions its own scanners: `check_scan.py` runs the pinned gitleaks installer before it scans in CI and runs the binary that installer names, so no workflow step or autofix hook carries that mapping a fourth time, the install path has one spelling, and a failed install aborts the lane with the installer's own reason. The scan baseline keeps a row for every scanner that ran with no findings, which is what stops a family whose rows all fell to zero from reading as brand new and seeding its growth unchecked. (Closes #1720, Refs #1722) |
| 0.895.0 | 2026-11-03 | GitHub intake stages 3 and 4 hold contributor code behind the owner's `run-ci` label and finish the loop on the GitHub side. An imported PR already carried `external` and `autofix:hold`; now `pr.yml`'s `gate` and `title` jobs and `plugins-live.yml` also require `run-ci`, ask for a disposable `external` runner, and skip the autofixer, `live` and bench jobs. No runner registers that label yet, so an admitted job queues and contributor code never runs: the design's fail-closed default. The hold is not bypassable from the head because intake denies every `.forgejo/` path and the import refuses to push unless the imported tree's `.forgejo/` is byte-equal to current main. The `title` job holds the repo token, so an external PR runs main's `scripts/` there. `just intake --run-ci <N>` admits a reviewed external PR: owner only, external only, an open PR, its audited imported commit still the same change (a base-only merge is allowed, a moved change is not) and `.forgejo/` equal to current main. `just intake --land <N>` comments `landed as <mirror-sha> on Forgejo` on the GitHub PR, gives the one-line rebase instruction and closes it, mapping the forge head through the mirror's commit map. Both run behind the transport seam and are tested with the fake transport only; the real transport's write half is not exercised here. `just intake --poll` is the write pass, while `.forgejo/workflows/intake.yml` runs the read-only one and is inert: `workflow_dispatch` only, its cron line present and commented, naming no secret. `docs/FORGE.md` gains the `external` runner, the hold's argument and a "GitHub contributors" section for the owner and Max; the runner requirements live in the ops handoff at `2026-10-29-intake34/ops-handoff.md` in the design directory. Max's login, email and key are left for the owner to add. (Refs #1719) |
| 0.894.0 | 2026-11-03 | An imported contributor's commits can name the identity that contributor chose. An optional `import_as` object in their `scripts/intake/allowed.json` entry, `name`, `email` and `consent`, rewrites the imported squash commit's author and its `Signed-off-by` trailer; `consent` records when and how they agreed, and an entry without it, or with an email that is not a plain address, refuses the import by naming the contributor and the field. The rewrite happens where the imported commit is produced and nowhere earlier, so the pinned email, the signature, the caps and every other check still judge the commits as submitted; a contributor with no `import_as` imports exactly as today, and the author is never the owner. Authorship stays honest and auditable. The rewrite moves no author date, message body or tree, the committer is still `yi-bot`, and the source account is kept in the import's audit comment rather than in the public commit objects: that record now names the GitHub login, the pull request URL, the original head sha and the verified signer's fingerprint, which is read from `verify-commit --raw` and only when the commit verified against the pinned signers. `just intake --dry-run` prints the identity it would apply. `docs/FORGE.md` states what the mapping does and does not do for the owner and the contributor. The fixture is a second contributor entry beside the recorded ones; `allowed.json` gains no contributor entry, because the owner adds those. (Refs #1794) |
| 0.893.0 | 2026-11-03 | GitHub intake stages 0 through 2 add real-producer fixtures, read-only dry-run decisions, strict signed checks, deterministic draft import and an offline real-git journey. The mirror rewrite now shares one helper with intake and retains its ephemeral commit map long enough to translate a mirror base. No schedule or contributor-code execution is enabled. `yi ask --no-tools` installs an empty tool table, so the intake lens receives only the fenced diff and cannot read the repository or call tools. The draft import always carries `external` and `autofix:hold`, never `run-ci`, `live` or `autofix`. The network check refuses URLs, IPs (a clock such as `12:30:45` and a MAC such as `aa:bb:cc` are not addresses: one needs `::` or all eight groups), `localhost`, hostnames on common TLDs and any ported, `@`-led, scp or rsync destination or command-argument hostname; a bare hostname on a rarer TLD, quoted or not, and a host on `rs`, `md`, `py` or `sh` off the well-known ports pass it, because `row.id`, `"response.failed"` and `main.rs:4444` read the same, and are left to the lens and the review. A refusal names every line it refused and an override carries all of them, so one approved line cannot waive a hit the owner never saw. Build-time execution covers a `build.rs`, a manifest's `build` key or `[build-dependencies]` section, a `proc-macro` crate and the vendored tree, which is denied whole. The dependency check reads both sides of a delete, so removing `Cargo.lock` is a dependency edit. Dry-run follows the open PR list to its last page instead of stopping at the first hundred, and every workflow installs git-filter-repo from `scripts/mirror/filter-repo.version`, its single spelling. The real transport carries no branch, draft or comment writes yet: they land with the stage-3 hold, and the journey drives the import through an injected transport. Runner hardening, owner CI admission and landing feedback remain stages 3 and 4. (D540; Refs #1719, Closes #1751) |
| 0.892.0 | 2026-11-03 | A reminder the model sets now fires when it says. `clock://at` takes an offset, and a time with none is local process-zone wall time, which the arming seam reads. A `file://` wait holds until the file exists. `block` refuses a time missing its scheme, a week- or day-relative word, and an address or a time left in `note` or `block`; any other op refuses `on`. A reminder that armed nothing can no longer read as set, and each refusal names an address the next call can use. The `todo` description says a reminder is a blocked todo and names the route for a repeating one, so the model stops reaching for the machine's crontab. (D539; Refs #1992) |
| 0.891.0 | 2026-11-03 | `yi models recommend [--role R]` suggests three models per role (high, medium, low) from OpenRouter's Artificial Analysis intelligence index and the catalog's prices, and prints the arithmetic behind each: `intelligence^exponent / blended $ per Mtok x popularity`. One table in `yi_ai::recommend::ROLES` sets each role's exponent, intelligence floor, context floor and token mix. The advisor squares intelligence, and the summarizer needs a 200k context. The primary and advisor blends come from the user's last 7 days of root-session tokens, so a 97.8% cache-hit history is priced mostly at the cache-read rate. Popularity from the previous day's OpenRouter top 50 is a multiplier capped at 1.03, so it cannot beat a clearly better ratio. Only OpenRouter models with an index, a credential and tool calling are scored, and the output counts the ones it dropped and why, after the CC BY 4.0 citation line and the snapshot's age. `yi models refresh` is the only fetch: three GETs with the user's OpenRouter key, at most once per 10 minutes, and a 429 names OpenRouter's limits and leaves the last snapshot in place. Nothing is applied to config. The bundled `models` skill tells the model when to suggest a change. The model browser that #1756 also asks for is not in this change. (Closes #1756) |
| 0.890.0 | 2026-11-03 | The queue tick now carries on when the recorder refuses. docs/ARCHITECTURE.md reached the 512000-byte blob cap, `record_changes.py` refused to write past it, and `record_main` ran it with `check=True`, so every tick that had a candidate to build crashed with `CalledProcessError` and nothing could land. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.889.0 | 2026-11-03 | The round-four answer to the bot on the Tree primitive. `stage::folds` is now the one spelling of the git-2.43 merge-tree gate, read by the fold itself, by the hand-back's merge-tree test and by the tree verbs, so a fourth copy cannot drift from the others, and `stage::ATTR_SOURCE_SINCE` is the one floor both roads read, since the fold and the hand-back both pass `--attr-source`; below 2.43 the tree-verb tests assert the refusal that names the floor, and a test that shims the host git to 2.34.1 runs that arm on every machine. `settle::status_row` is the one reader of a `git status --porcelain -z` record, which the fork's parent-state walk and the settle path list both call, so the rename rule has one owner; the merge into main took main's name for it and kept this branch's rename skip. `tree.discard` has its half that matters: a child's tree is refused by name and still stands after the refusal. The classifier's protected branch is read through `protected_branches`, whose test runs a `master` trunk and then a `main` one, so hardcoding `main` fails. (Closes #1941) |
| 0.888.0 | 2026-11-03 | `rlm.tree()` names a set of files, committed or not. `await t.fork("fix")` copies them cheaply, uncommitted files included, and `rlm.run(brief, tree=f)` gives a child that tree. `await f.check(cmd)` runs in a warm directory and answers green, red, abstain or still running. `await t.merge(f)` folds another tree's files in with no commit, and a tree that conflicted moves nothing. `await f.head()` names the files now, for `rewind` and `fork(at=)`. `await f.diff()` lists changes and the paths a merge would stop on. `read("tree://fix/")` lists them with no kernel, from any role. `isolation='worktree'` is unchanged for plan documents and for children whose results should land as commits. `rlm.merge_worktree` refuses a child on a forked tree, since a commit would claim a person's edits as the child's. (D538; Closes #1941) |
| 0.887.0 | 2026-11-03 | A tool call that writes or runs now leaves a `custom{tree_snapshot}` entry naming the tree it left, captured on a background thread so the tool never waits. Calls during a capture coalesce into one more. `/undo` reads none of these and still goes back to the turn's start. A capture past 500 ms, or killed at 5 s, turns per-call snapshots off for that tree, since background git must not load a checkout too large for it. Each killed capture clears its own lock first. (D537; Closes #1940) |
| 0.886.0 | 2026-11-03 | A child's `check=` now runs in a warm directory rather than in the child's own tree. `merge_worktree` settles first and checks the settled commit, so a job still running refuses it at once, and a check's unignored writes no longer land with the child's work. `rlm.result` on a fork checks a snapshot of the tree, and a plan's verifier claims from the same pool. A check past its budget or its queue reads as an abstention, never as a red, and the reply names the bound. A check that writes or edits frees its directory all the same. A warm directory keeps only cargo's bookkeeping. `.venv` and `node_modules` go between checks: a check could otherwise pass on what the last borrower installed, and only cargo re-verifies what it keeps. A repository that is not cargo pays a full install per check. (D536; Closes #1939) |
| 0.885.0 | 2026-11-03 | A shadow tick that finds its candidate already in main now keeps the ref, so the next `on` tick still closes the PR's issues and clears its label. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.884.0 | 2026-11-02 | A commit message that the public mirror would refuse now fails the branch's guardrails instead of stopping the mirror after the merge. `check_public_surface.py` reads the messages on `HEAD --not` the branch's fork, applies `replace.txt` as the mirror does, and matches them against `deny.txt`. A hit names the commit, and the refusal says to reword it or add a literal. Author and committer identities are still judged only by the mirror job, since this does not model `mailmap.txt`. (Closes #1984) |
| 0.883.0 | 2026-11-02 | The README is commands first: every section opens with the block a reader copies, and the prose under it is two lines or none (Closes #1861). It is 7208 bytes where it was 10804, 66.7 percent, with the same twelve sections and the same capabilities, no paragraph longer than two lines and none of the opening prose restating its heading. A new contract test, `readme_fenced_commands_name_verbs_help_prints`, reads every fenced `yi <verb>` in the README and fails when `yi --help` prints no such verb, so a renamed verb cannot leave a command a reader copies into an error. |
| 0.882.0 | 2026-11-02 | `rlm.merge_worktree` now merges in the object store, through `git merge-tree --write-tree`, so a hand-back takes no lane and writes no checkout. It reads attributes from the parent's commit, as `git merge` in a checkout of that commit would. A git older than 2.43, or a version that cannot be read, takes the old road; the plan engine stays on it, since it verifies in that directory. A repository's `pre-merge-commit` and `commit-msg` hooks no longer fire on this path, and a repository with `commit.gpgsign` gets an unsigned integration commit, as the host's other commits already are. (D535; Closes #1938) |
| 0.881.0 | 2026-11-02 | The merge queue's tick now stops with the forge's own words when a listing comes back as an error, instead of dying on an AttributeError that never said what came back. A refused issue close now keeps the candidate ref, so the next tick tries again, where before the issue stayed open with nothing left to retry. After four hours the tick says so on the PR and moves on, so one issue it may not close cannot hold up the queue. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.880.0 | 2026-11-02 | The autofix pass now takes `queue`-labelled PRs before anything else, oldest first after them. A pass fixes at most five PRs from the oldest end of the open list, so a conflicted PR the queue handed over could wait behind dozens of older ones it had no reason to wait for. A queued PR the fixer hands off now leaves the queue and says so, because the queue would otherwise ask the fixer again on every build. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.879.0 | 2026-11-02 | A worktree child no longer claims a lane slot. It takes a linked worktree of its own under `~/.yi/forks`, cut at the parent's `HEAD`: a directory clone on APFS, a plain checkout elsewhere or when the parent has submodules. Sessions keep their lanes. `lanes.slots` no longer bounds children; family caps do, and `family.cap` tops out at 129, since the root's kernel counts as a live session and 128 means 127 children otherwise. A child's first writing command carries the third-party cargo units from the parent's `target/debug`, those whose dep-info names nothing in its tree, then syncs the toolchain and runs a committed `.yi/setup`. A package with one unit that names the tree loses all of them, because a clone at a new path otherwise reads Fresh and tests its parent. `.worktreeinclude` and `carry=[...]` on `rlm.run` copy files that are both listed and ignored, and `.venv` never carries. Discarding a child stops the jobs still running in its tree. (D534; Closes #1937) |
| 0.878.0 | 2026-11-02 | Contained commands on Linux now run under Landlock. A contained command starts as `yi __contain`, which restricts itself and execs: writes reach its roots, reads reach the system roots, home past the credential stores, and its writable roots. A kernel that refuses the ruleset makes `Contain` an ask, as a machine with no sandbox did before. Landlock only grants, so a `config` inside a writable root cannot be denied. The host saves what its next git reads when it builds a profile, compares before each of its gits, puts back what changed and refuses that one call. There is no network wall on Linux. A lane's own branch stays writable under `yi/`; any other branch and `git stash` refuse, since neither leaf has a parent the rules may name. (D533; Closes #602) |
| 0.877.0 | 2026-11-02 | Checkpoints now keep one store per repository, each worktree with its own index, rather than a store per path. A new child no longer pays a fresh store on its first tool batch, and `.git/info/exclude` now reaches a checkpoint. An old session's `/undo` still reads the per-path store, which ages out as before. (D532; Closes #1936) |
| 0.876.0 | 2026-11-02 | Moves with no behaviour change, so the files past their line cap have room for the tree work: `BranchName`, `Sha` and `Head` to `lane/names.rs`, a kernel child's hand-back to `lane/handback.rs`, the spawn-kwarg types to `subagent/build.rs`, and `job_profile` to `kernel_state.rs`. (Closes #1935) |
| 0.875.0 | 2026-11-02 | `fgj` and `gh` now ask and an approval runs outside the sandbox, since a contained one has no login. An allow rule in `permissions.allow` runs them outside unasked, unless the call aims `-R`, `--repo`, `--hostname` or `--config` somewhere that is not this project's origin, which asks whatever the rule says. `sandbox.hostRecipes` in the home config, such as `[{"call": "just pr", "pin": ["justfile", "scripts"]}]`, runs one command outside unasked while every pinned path matches the trunk on origin with nothing untracked. Otherwise it asks, naming the pin: a recipe a branch rewrote would run on the host. An empty `pin` list asks too, since it pins nothing, and a subagent's broker meets the same recipes its parent does. A compound never matches, so nothing rides out behind it. `just pr open` now refuses an unpushed branch instead of pushing, since a host-run push would build a child's `build.rs` outside the sandbox. (D531; Closes #1934) |
| 0.874.0 | 2026-11-02 | A contained `git fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote` and `clone` now run with no question, and so does `git push` to any branch but trunk, `main` or `master`. The host refreshes a mirror before a command that names a read, and forwards what a contained push left in an outbox after the command ends, with no hooks and never in the outbox. `--force`, `--delete`, `+x` and `:x` still ask and leave. A push to main asks with a diffstat; headless it is refused and origin stays put. What happened reaches the model as a row: `[pushed feature to origin: …]`, `[not pushed: origin/feature has commits this lacks; merge, or git push --force asks]`, `[not pushed: main asks a person, who declined or is not here: +300 -20 in 7 files]`, or `[push of feature unknown after 120 s: it may have landed; git fetch and compare before pushing again]`. A remote's own words are fenced and redacted. An origin URL holding a credential gets no lane. Its git has transports off by config, so an ordinary push fails contained and the next call asks; a process that removes that config can still leave through a seeded host, which the trust table records. `refs/remotes/origin` is host-only in a main checkout now, so a moved `origin/HEAD` cannot make a real main push skip its ask. (D530; Closes #1933) |
| 0.873.0 | 2026-11-02 | `cargo add`, `cargo fetch`, `npm`, `pnpm`, `yarn`, `pip`, `uv` and `bun` installs now run contained in `auto` with no question, and in `ask` an approval stays contained. A global install (`-g`, `--user`, `--target`, `--prefix`, `--root`), `cargo install`, `publish`, `login`, `link`, `brew` and `gem` still ask, and an approval runs outside. `%pip install` works in a kernel. The kernel venv now carries pip, about 6.5 MB, and a cell's install goes to the session's own `site` directory, which is on its path. Existing venvs rebuild once, since the bootstrap schema moved. The prompts stop telling a model to ask before installing. (D529; Closes #1932) |
| 0.872.0 | 2026-11-02 | `yi plugin add agent-browser` gives the model a browser by bash, with no new tool, and makes true the sentence agent-desktop's browser refusal already says. The obvious copy of agent-desktop was probed and refused: a Chrome outside the sandbox read a planted `~/.ssh` key, and a contained `curl` drove it over its loopback debug port to reach the web. So the daemon stays outside, since the sandbox bans its unix socket, and Chrome goes inside a sandbox of its own through a script `add` writes. The default session's Chrome has loopback alone, so a contained program reaching it gains nothing and nothing asks. `--session web` adds the internet by address, with one yes per session. A repo's `agent-browser.json` is never read, and the daemon goes after five idle minutes rather than an hour. (D528; Refs #1958) |
| 0.871.0 | 2026-11-02 | `grep` counts a file the read gate refuses and names it in one `[…]` row instead of answering "No matches found". A search over a key store, a device or the workspace `.git` never opened the file, so the old answer told the model a guarded store held no match when nothing had looked, which the loud-caps law forbids (045). The count and the reason are one row; nothing opens those paths, so the row names no continuation call. (Closes #1054) |
| 0.870.0 | 2026-11-02 | This PR hardens drive and tool-cell tests in two units, in commit order: HD-V05-console-oracle and HD-V03-real-producer-fixtures. HD-V05-console-oracle. Console `wait-frame` needles now match within one rendered row instead of across the frame's serialized rows. The new `wait-row` step matches a whole trimmed row. A failed solo `wait-frame` or `wait-row` stops the script instead of running later input on the wrong screen. HD-V03-real-producer-fixtures. The ceiling-nudge test replays metadata recorded from real bash calls. A yi-cli integration test feeds real read, grep and edit results to the TUI's cell rendering, and console permission drives use scrubbed frames recorded from a real worker for reject, close-with-ask and a second ask. (Closes #1504, Refs #1442) |
| 0.869.0 | 2026-11-02 | A plan verifier's case runner now reads a sandbox refusal before the cap that cuts its output, so a refused runner abstains instead of reading as the product's `output_overflow`. A refusal that ends a run no longer unsays a case the product had already failed. A child's contained check is now tested by driving it to its result, not by its spawn returning. (Closes #1931) |
| 0.868.0 | 2026-11-02 | Shadow no longer writes main and announces each PR head once, so it stops gating the oldest PR forever and reaches the rest of the queue. Under `on`, a recorder push refused for any reason but a moved main no longer stops the queue from building. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.867.0 | 2026-11-02 | Yi's own git calls now go through `yi_tools::host_command`, and every other program it runs on the host resolves the same way: to an absolute path outside the trees a contained process can write. Before this, a tree's `bin` on `PATH`, as direnv or an activated venv leaves it, ran a contained write on the host's next `git status`, and so did a gitlink planted with a `.git` pointer, which sent that status into a child git reading the planted config. A path someone configured, such as a relative `lanes.land`, is held to the same rule: inside a tree a contained process writes, it is refused. A hand-back now commits past the repository's hooks and signing, with a fallback identity, so a red `pre-commit` no longer strands a child's work; a red surfaces at `check=`, the next `just commit` or the forge. Untracked `target/`, `node_modules/`, `.venv/`, top-level `dist/` and `build/` stay out of a candidate. A `HEAD` planted on trunk or on a branch checked out elsewhere is refused rather than committed to. Plumbing a caller parses is read whole or fails naming the cut: a long `git status -z` lost its middle, and a dirty file with it. The lane code now reads trunk from origin's `HEAD` instead of assuming `main`. (D527; Closes #1520) |
| 0.866.0 | 2026-11-02 | A lane can no longer touch trunk, a sibling's branch or `packed-refs`. Its sandbox writes its admin dir, the shared objects, its own branch and the stash. The branch comes from the host's record of the slot, and a tree whose record does not parse gets none, since `HEAD` itself is contained-writable. A new local branch, a tag, `git worktree add`, LFS and rerere are refused in a lane. The refusal now asks on the next call, and an approval runs the command outside; before, `git switch -c x` repeated the same refusal forever and nothing asked. A contained git no longer runs auto maintenance, which printed a harmless `packed-refs.lock` error on commit. A grant of `$HOME`, `~/.yi` or a credential store is refused whether or not the host builds a Seatbelt profile, so `permissions.allowWrite` on one exits 2 on every host instead of on a Mac alone. A wipe that could not read the tree after it reports that failure, and a survivor is named for what held it: a submodule's worktree or a directory `clean -fd` left in place. (D526; Closes #1930) |
| 0.865.0 | 2026-11-02 | The merge queue's tick now records whatever main has landed before it builds a candidate. A recorder job waiting on a runner pushed while one candidate went green, which moved main and threw that gate run away. The later job now finds nothing to push. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.864.0 | 2026-11-02 | Edit and grep are proven against a swapped link deterministically rather than by racing a thread: a test seam on the gate swaps `d` to a link to the key store just before each open the tool makes, and the test pins how many opens that is, so a tool that skips the gate for an open fails on the count; a tool that reopens by path after the gate does not. The two race tests they replace could miss a broken guard in a run, and they took about 10 s between them where these take tens of milliseconds each. No behaviour changes. (Closes #1988) |
| 0.863.0 | 2026-11-02 | The forge can now land reviewed PRs by itself. A tick every five minutes merges the oldest `queue`-labelled PR onto current main on `queue/candidate`, reads the gate on that commit, and fast-forwards main to it, so nobody updates a branch only to test it. It stays off until the repository variable `MERGE_QUEUE` says `shadow` or `on`. `just pr queue N` enqueues a PR, and `just pr merge --now` forces one past the queue. This takes one PR at a time; batches come next. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.862.0 | 2026-11-02 | `just pr status`, `just pr merge` and the autofix bot now read a head's lanes from its commit statuses instead of the last sixty rows of the task list. That list has no row for a job still waiting on a runner, so a rerun read as its old failure, and sixty rows is an hour or two of forge activity. `just pr rerun` also refuses while a gate lane is pending, because a second reopen cancels the waiting run and writes failure on every context; the bot takes that refusal as waiting and keeps its one rerun on the head. (Refs #1966) |
| 0.861.0 | 2026-11-02 | The public mirror had not moved since 2026-10-29, because its history scan refused two things the rewrite left in place. The widened home-path rule also matched a spelling with no leading hyphen in two old revisions of a plan, and `replace.txt` rewrote only the hyphen form. Two commit messages quoted the mirror test's planted address, and the tree gate reads no messages. `replace.txt` now rewrites both, with the address rules matched on the quoted text alone, so `sync_test.sh`'s planted address still trips the deny rule. No deny rule changed. (Closes #1982) |
| 0.860.0 | 2026-11-02 | The console no longer loses a request it queued just before quitting. On quit, the reader thread closed the shared socket as soon as it noticed shutdown, which could beat the writer thread to the last queued line, so a sidebar toggle pressed right before quitting was sometimes never saved. The writer now closes the socket after its queue is flushed. (Closes #1970) |
| 0.859.0 | 2026-11-01 | The six link-swap race tests in `crates/runtime/tests/permission_scope.rs` waited a fixed eight seconds each and gave up on a call count nobody checked; they now run a bounded number of calls whose swap is timed to land at a different point of each one, and stop when enough swaps landed between the broker's check and the tool's open, so their wall follows the work instead of the clock. Each call's swap is armed on a deadline the swapper thread waits for and the test times against a moving average of the check and the call taken from the rounds themselves, swept over sixty-four offsets, so every window between a tool's check and its act is reached without a sleep holding the test open. A swap counts only when its link stood, it landed after the check and it changed what the tool returned, since one landing after the open leaves the output undisturbed. A test now fails when too few swaps land in that window, since a run that never raced proves nothing. (Closes #1723) |
| 0.858.0 | 2026-11-01 | `yi plugin add agent-desktop` lets the model read and operate Mac apps through their accessibility trees, by bash, with no new tool. The sandbox refuses the program, even its `version`, so only its plain name leaves it, through a table of verbs and flags in `yi_permission`. A yes covers one app for the session, whether the command names it with `--app` or holds a ref, whose app comes from the record the snapshot wrote. A terminal, an editor, System Settings or a password app, and any verb or flag the table does not name, asks every time and keeps nothing. A browser is refused in every mode and pointed at agent-browser. So is whatever takes the user's own mouse, keyboard or front app (`--headed`, `launch --activate`, `press`, `hover`, `drag`, `mouse-*`, `double-click`, `batch`): the owner, 2026-10-31, "needs to not interrupt my mouse it needs to be its own agent mouse". `session start --cursor` runs unasked and draws an agent cursor upstream keeps apart from the real one. What the program prints returns inside the `yi-external` fence, and a review model never answers for it. The row's new `host` shape keeps it out of `yi plugin add --all` and the eval containers, and Apple silicon Macs are the only target, since no other build was run. The Python client is deleted, which the owner approved on 2026-10-13: "remove python client". §1.2's clause on browser automation, computer use and non-coding capabilities goes with it. (D525; Refs #1958) |
| 0.857.0 | 2026-11-01 | A change file now carries `ledger+`, `ledger~`, `ledger=` and `ledger-` lines, which the recorder applies to the feature ledger after the merge, so two PRs editing one row no longer conflict. A branch that edits a row directly is refused and shown the lines to write, unless the edit was committed before this landed. A line that no longer applies is named on the recorder's output and its text kept on the row. (D524; Refs #1966) |
| 0.856.0 | 2026-11-01 | `just check` no longer goes red on macOS for three reasons unrelated to the diff under test. The CLI surface tests clear every provider key from the spawned binary's environment, so a shell's `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` cannot turn the keyless doctor row into a live probe. Drive tests name their scratch dir without the test name, which had pushed one socket past the 104-byte `sun_path` limit. The spared-root sandbox test now spells its spare `/tmp/...` against a root at `/private/tmp/...`, a real second spelling, where its old guard compared a canonical path with itself. (Closes #1961) |
| 0.855.0 | 2026-11-01 | Yi's replies are asked for as list-first terse prose: the identity prompt's Voice section now opens with short sentences, the answer first, and one numbered or bulleted line per point, with prose around a list capped at two short paragraphs, detail only when it is asked for, no restating of the question and no closing summary, and brevity never drops an error, a warning or an undone item. The sentence naming a banned tell is the whole ban list now: the wiki tells (#1862) that were spread over four paragraphs sit in one `## Machine writing, all banned` section, which is 1,398 bytes shorter than the section it replaces. `evals/prose.py` scores a saved answer set and gates the prompt. It reports mean sentence length, the share of multi-point answers that use a list, mean paragraphs, hits per tell and rule mentions, so a before/after pair is numbers rather than an impression, and `--gate` fails when the shipped prompt stops stating one of the nine rules, spells a tell outside its ban list, or scores a word the ban list does not name. The selfcheck is hand-counted: one life-sized pair under `evals/fixtures/prose/` and the degenerate answers an empty cell or a fenced block leaves. It runs inside `just check` for no spend; the 25-question DeepSeek V4.1 before/after the issue asks for is a paid lane and did not run here, so #1862 stays open for it. (D523; Refs #1862) |
| 0.854.0 | 2026-11-01 | The orphan gate (`scripts/guardrails/check_orphans.py`) now counts a read only where code reads: `.name` in production source, or a name a destructuring pattern binds (`let S { a, .. } = s`, a match arm, a `for` pattern, a `..` rest pattern, a thiserror `{field}` interpolation). A struct or variant literal that sets a field is no longer a read, and a test is no longer a reader. Enum variant fields are scanned like struct fields, and a `pub struct` or `pub enum` in yi-types that no production code names outside its own definition is reported as dead. `--selfcheck` carries the three shapes the issue names (a field only a struct literal sets, a variant field only a variant literal sets, an unused yi-types shape) plus one row per destructuring form, and fails with each scan and each pattern test disabled in turn. What the stricter scan found is deleted: `ViewState::split_borders`, `Preparation::messages_to_summarize` and `turn_prefix_messages`, `Connection::info`, `Relevance::unsupplied`, the unused `AcpNotification` and `AdvisoryOutcome` types, and the lever manifest's `Spec` min/max/tunable fields are now what `Levers::set` validates against, so the table is read by the loader and not only by a test. (Closes #1585) |
| 0.853.0 | 2026-11-01 | The autofixer's model and the PR code it runs now work as a throwaway user, one per fix, and root only pushes. Before, a PR's build, test or hook and the model's `--yolo` commands ran as root beside the fixer: on the real runner that code read the parent's environment, which holds the forge token, and wrote the fixer's `~/.yi/skills` and its target dir, so one PR could steer the next PR's fix. `pr_autofix.in_worker` re-runs the script under a numeric uid that owns only its own workspace, with copies of the warm target dir, the cargo downloads (never its credentials) and `~/.yi`, and with no variable named like a token. A commit comes back as a bundle that root fetches as data and pushes, an exception comes back as JSON read through a table, and when the worker ends everything left under its uid is killed. `lock_job` first closes the checkout's `.git/config` and the runner's mode-666 step files. A job that is not root refuses to run in CI. Outside CI, `just pr autofix` keeps the user that ran it. The OpenRouter key stays in the worker, because the model needs it, so PR code can still read that key. (Closes #1955) |
| 0.852.0 | 2026-11-01 | A contained command now has the network, through a loopback proxy that decides by host name. Registries and GitHub are seeded, `sandbox.egress` in the home config adds to them, "always" keeps a grant, and any other host asks a person once per spawn; headless, it is refused. The proxy reads no traffic: it tunnels, so TLS stays end to end. A raw connect is still refused by the sandbox, so a client that ignores the proxy variables fails rather than leaving unseen. What the proxy refused reaches the model as a row beside the output: `[egress: refused example.com: not seeded or in sandbox.egress; asked you, retry after your answer]`. Bash, background jobs, the kernel's `bash()`, a kernel cell and a contained check all carry it. A contained install writes `~/.cargo/{registry,git}`, `~/.cache/uv`, `~/.npm`, pnpm, pip and bun's stores, wherever `CARGO_HOME` and `UV_CACHE_DIR` put them, but never `.cargo/bin` or a config. This opens one thing on purpose: a contained process can edit a `build.rs` in the registry that your next terminal build runs. It is the price of installs that work, and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` carries it with the other residuals. `sanitize` also strips bidi format controls, so a hostile name in a row or an ask reads in the order its bytes run. (D522; Closes #601) |
| 0.851.0 | 2026-11-01 | A contained process can no longer write the git files the host reads as authority. With `extensions.worktreeConfig` on, `config.worktree` was writable, and the host's next `git status` ran a `core.fsmonitor` planted there; a lane's `.git` pointer could send that status to a config the process wrote. `info`, a submodule's config, `objects/info/alternates` and a sibling lane's admin dir had the same shape. Work inside a submodule still commits: only its host-read files are denied. The wall sits on the two directories that hold those names, `<git>/worktrees` and `<git>/modules`, not on the names one build found there: a lane keeps its own admin dir and the module dirs it already has, and an admin dir or a module created after the profile is built is under the seal. `--allow-write` is judged at or below a guarded path only, so granting the checkout root in a repository is taken again; a refusal still names the tree an approval would widen. The deny outranks every grant. A contained `git sparse-checkout`, `git config --worktree` and `git worktree add` from a main checkout are now refused, and `git init` in a directory that is no repository asks once. (D521; Closes #1929) |
| 0.850.0 | 2026-11-01 | A check now runs where the same command would run in bash: inside the sandbox, outside yolo. Before, a goal check, a child's `check=`, a plan verifier and a schedule's `exec://` source all ran on the host, so `cargo test` as a check ran a child's `build.rs` outside the sandbox. A check the gate would only contain used to be refused at spawn; it now runs contained, so `just check` can be one. A contained check has no unix sockets, no writes outside its tree and, until the egress proxy lands, no network past loopback. What the sandbox stopped is named: a goal or child check opens its evidence with a `[contained: …]` row, and a plan verifier abstains with the same words instead of failing. (D520; Closes #1931) |
| 0.849.0 | 2026-11-01 | A review round no longer reads a head again when no fix has touched it. One refuter seat that did not answer used to mark the whole round unread, so every lens read the head afresh and the next round re-read the whole PR; that now re-reads nothing, and the round's findings stand on the votes that came. A lost lens reads the head again alone. Merging the base re-reads nothing unless a line the PR adds or removes changed, where before a base edit beside one of its lines counted as new code. The reason a call went unanswered is now in the job log. (Closes #1967) |
| 0.848.0 | 2026-11-01 | A shell fence in a finished answer of a console chat pane now ends its header row in a `▶ run` chip, the way Claude Code offers a command to run. A press on the chip types the block into the terminal pane the person is looking at, or the one that tab's chips fed last, or opens one beside the chat in the session's own working directory, and presses enter, so the person sees the command run in their own shell and the agent is told nothing. Fences labelled `bash`, `sh`, `shell` and `zsh` get a chip; any other fence, and a paragraph that spells the chip, do not. `Theme.run` says a surface can press a chip: only the console's chat panes set it, so the solo transcript, the markdown and diff viewers and a child's card draw none. An answer is rendered whole once its message is sealed, because the growing render cannot tell a closed fence from an open one; a chip appears when the message ends, never on a fence still arriving. `markdown.rs` gave its `Settled` streaming cursor to `markdown/settled.rs`, its chip helpers live in `markdown/run.rs`, `app.rs` gave `pane_row` and the new chip lookup to `app/pane.rs`, and the console's `Hit` table moved to `hit.rs`, each a split at a seam so no file past its ceiling grew. (Closes #1949) |
| 0.847.0 | 2026-11-01 | An eval run can compact without a summarizer: `compaction.render` (0..=1, tunable, default 0) makes `Compactor::maybe_compact` compose the checkpoint from the `<yi_compact_view>` and the file lists that a summary would have been prefixed with anyway, and it opens no summarizer stream at all (Closes #1574). Nothing else about a compaction changes: the same cut, the same retained tail, the same `details`, and the elision path is untouched. The default stays 0, because the kill test that would flip it keeps planted identifiers per compaction over at least ten compactions a mode and needs the paid provider; no run here measured it, so the lever ships off and the measurement is still owed. |
| 0.846.0 | 2026-11-01 | A red test that passes once the base is merged in now takes that merge, with no model turn, because the head only lacks what the base landed. The fixer tries the merge in a scratch worktree and pushes it only when the failing tests are red there before it and green after, so a flake or a head merely behind a busy main still goes to the model. The daemon now runs settings requests in the order they arrive. Saves ran side by side, so a quick double press of the sidebar chord could leave the older state in `config.json`, and the console would open on it next time. (Closes #1944) |
| 0.845.0 | 2026-11-01 | The compaction retention floor now keeps only the words the user typed (and a child's task request, which the model also reads bare). Host notices, job and mail announcements, `<yi_internal_context>` wrapped reminders and earlier compaction summaries are no longer carried into the kept tail as if the user had said them; the summary covers them. `retain_floor` asks `Attribution::reads_as_typed`, the same test the request converter uses, so a message stored before attribution existed still counts as typed. The stored session is unchanged: a cut is a view over the ledger. (Closes #1758) |
| 0.844.0 | 2026-11-01 | `read` and `grep` on an `mcp://` address now hand the model the server's text inside the same `yi-external` data fence a web search arrives in, with `mcp://<server>/<resource>` as its source and the session's screen applied. Before, the server's JSON came back bare, so a hostile resource could not be told from the agent's own output. A program's `fetch` of the same address keeps the bare text, since code parses it; `yi mcp` output in a cell or bash is a separate seam this change leaves alone. (Closes #1946) |
| 0.843.0 | 2026-11-01 | A session runs one compaction at a time. `Compactor::maybe_compact` takes the session's in-flight flag with a compare-and-swap before it spends anything, and a request that arrives while another compaction is summarizing returns at once with `Compacted::Busy`: it makes no summarizer call, takes neither the pending request nor its one-shot instructions, and writes no history. Before, only the flag's value was shared — both callers set it and either one's end cleared it — so an idle `/compact` mid-summarization and a turn crossing its own threshold could each read the window, call the summarizer and advance the window and the history on their own, and whichever finished last won a write the other had already made stale. `AgentSession::compact_now` reports the refusal as `CompactOutcome::Busy`, whose RPC frame reads `applied: false, scheduled: true` because `/compact` stays pending and the next boundary runs it; the loop's own boundary simply keeps its history and carries on. `Compactor::compacting`, which the scheduler reads to defer a heartbeat, is now true for exactly as long as one compaction runs rather than until the first of two finishes. (Closes #553) |
| 0.842.0 | 2026-11-01 | The review bot and the autofixer can read the owner's skills, which a job's HOME never held: asked to name its skills, a `yi ask` with the job's HOME and clone answered NONE. `just install-skills` now copies `.ruler/skills` beside `skills/yi` into `~/.yi/skills`, `review.yml` and `autofix.yml` run it from their main checkout, and `.ruler/skills` gains `ponytail`, `caveman`, `har-io` and `har-target` so that main holds them. When a change touches Rust, a lens or fixer prompt opens by making `har` and `har-api` its first two reads, and it lists the other HAR skills with the trigger the catalog's 60-character clip would cut. The prompts use absolute paths because a PR's `.yi/skills/har` shadows `yi://skills/har` in the bot's checkout. The clone's own code runs under a HOME of its own, so a PR's build, test or commit hook cannot rewrite the skills a later prompt reads. Refuters read no skill: they test whether a claim holds in the code. A round's or a fix's footer now says which skills it read, so a job that lost the install shows `skills read none`. The gate runs `just install-skills` into a temp directory and refuses one that leaves a skill the repository ships out of the tree a prompt opens, so a broken copy fails a check instead of the bots' next round. (Closes #1928) |
| 0.841.0 | 2026-10-31 | The autofixer's stops now say what stopped it. A PR whose failing tests also fail at its base gets a `base-red` comment and no label, and one issue names those tests, since nothing else reports that main is red. What no model can change gets a `handoff` comment naming the hour it tries again, also with no label. A lone overrun of the stored binary ceiling lands as its own `Ratchet:` commit without a model turn. `autofix:failed` now means a fix was tried and fell short. A hidden sidebar keeps its way back on screen when a banner or a connection line takes the hint row: with no sessions the footer read only "no sessions for this root — alt+n starts one", and #1924's removal of the header label left nothing naming the chord. That row now ends in "⌥b sidebar", which also turns main's test gate green. (D519; Closes #1926) |
| 0.840.0 | 2026-10-31 | A hidden console sidebar no longer draws its `⌥b sidebar` hint at the left of the header row. The hint row at the bottom already names the chord and word, so the header's copy was a second place for the same fact and it pushed the workspace tabs and the four pane buttons right by its width. The tabs and buttons now start at the header's left edge in every sidebar mode, and their hit rectangles match what is drawn. (Closes #1923) |
| 0.839.0 | 2026-10-31 | The mirror pushes one ref at a time. GitHub refused the first run with the new token (GH013: pushes can not update more than 2 branches or tags) because `scripts/mirror/sync.sh` sent main and both moved tags in one push; it now pushes main, then each tag, and `sync_test.sh`'s scratch GitHub repo refuses a push that updates more than two refs. (Closes #1792) |
| 0.838.0 | 2026-10-31 | The public mirror never carries `scripts/intake/allowed.json`, the owner's contributor list. `scripts/mirror/exclude.txt` drops it at every commit, `deny.txt` refuses any fox.com address in a tree that reaches the scan, and `sync_test.sh` fails when the file reaches the scratch mirror. (Closes #1797) |
| 0.837.0 | 2026-10-31 | The classifier now runs on TypeSafe's Jev through OpenRouter. `yi setup` saves `models.classifier: typesafe/jev-1.13` once an OpenRouter key exists and saves nothing without one; nothing is installed and `yi serve` starts no sidecar. A `models.classifier` that still names a retired Laya checkpoint (`english`) loads but does not run, and the refusal names `typesafe/jev-1.13` as its replacement: repointing it would bill a remote model the owner never chose. A configured `classifier.url` is honoured only at the provider's own host over TLS or on this machine, so the account key cannot reach a third party, and `yi setup` clears the Laya era URL it used to leave behind. `yi doctor` asks the endpoint one question with a short deadline and reports a dead one instead of a green row over an unreachable host; the row says that typed messages, judged commands and the working directory leave the machine. Jev's `choice` answer carries `probabilities` and `confidence`; the skill pointer reads the chosen option's probability when `answer_confidence` is absent. The approval question is the V2 question the measurement scored, each value capped at 1000 chars with the shared `[kept … cap …]` row naming the cap, and a recent call sent as its bash command or its tool and path, never the file body it carried and a judged command past 4000 chars left to the person. Approval allows at 0.8 (`allowAt`) and 0.9 for a destructive command (`allowDestructiveAt`). An unreachable endpoint keeps the breaker: skill pointers fall silent for 60 s and approval falls to the reviewer or the person. `LAYA_API_KEY` and the minted `laya` key are gone. (D518; Closes #1784) |
| 0.836.0 | 2026-10-31 | The console's sidebar had two states, the full list and a narrow avatar rail, and no way to get it out of the way on a small terminal. ⌥b and ⌘B now walk three: rail, full, hidden. A hidden sidebar is worth no columns at all, so the panes take them; it draws no rows, so focus cannot sit in it and tab never lands there, and the header keeps its way back as `⌥b sidebar` at the left, where the tabs start after it. The state a press lands on is written to `console.sidebar` in config.json through `_yi/set_default`, so the next console starts in it; the settings pane lists the same leaf, restarts-only, beside the title options. The palette and the keys sheet carry the one chord, so the third state needs no second binding. (Closes #1713) |
| 0.835.0 | 2026-10-31 | Terminal text keeps its existing character reveal. Console panes choose one-row sliding, half-page jumps, or a stationary view with a hidden-row marker; the inline viewport keeps its unpaced commits, and the six tests that defended its held window went with it (`solo_still_marks_rows_hidden_from_the_inline_viewport`, `solo_follow_levels_pace_the_rows_that_land`, `a_slide_frame_lands_what_it_held_without_another_event`, `a_paced_release_keeps_the_prompt_zone_whole`, `a_submitted_prompt_releases_the_inline_still_hold`, `a_resumed_transcript_lands_in_one_frame`); `still_holds_the_view_and_marks_hidden_rows_at_a_nonzero_offset` keeps the marker's reveal in the pane. Ordinary running tool cards reserve bounded collapsed result space, child cards keep their bounded four-row answer window, and notebook updates preserve a reader's scroll position. Fixed-clock frames measured `slide` as 22 one-row moves, `page` as a 15-row jump and a 5-row final step, and `still` as five zero-move frames with its hidden-row marker. The live settings choice reaches visible and parked chats, while macOS Reduce Motion selects `still` only when config is unset; its preference plist is read directly so the launch path spawns no helper process, and the reader is compiled for the macOS arm alone. A card row past the rows it keeps is elided with kept-of-total rows and its size, and the verbose view and a failed call's reason keep more of the line before it elides. (D514, D515, D516, D517; Closes #1711) |
| 0.834.0 | 2026-10-31 | A turn checkpoint captured the session's own home whenever the work tree held it. `yi --here` in a scratch directory whose `HOME` sat under that directory staged the home into `~/.yi/checkpoints` on every turn start and turn end, so anything under the home that changed between turns was stored again: measured on the unfixed binary with a home inside the work tree, 5 MB of churn under it added 5 MB to the shadow repo on every turn. A kernel venv rebuilt in that home is a few hundred megabytes, and eighty short requests filled a 48 GB tmpfs. A capture now leaves out the home the store serves when that home lies inside the work tree, which is where the runtime keys the store (`<home>/.yi/checkpoints`); in a session whose home is the work tree itself, `yi` started in `~`, that home cannot be spared whole without sparing the tree, so the capture spares `~/.yi` and the caches beside it (`~/.cache`, `~/.local`, `~/.cargo`) and keeps the rest of the tree; it costs undo coverage of `~/.yi` outside the store in that invocation, and a cache directory outside those three names is still captured. (Closes #262) |
| 0.833.0 | 2026-10-31 | The console's Files button opens a tree of the workspace instead of the command palette: the popup lists the focused root folded by folder, a click or ⏎ on a folder folds it, a click or ⏎ on a file opens it in an editor pane and closes the tree, and ← → walk out and in with the cursor kept on a row the frame draws. `.git` and every path a `.gitignore` declares stay out, judged by the same matcher the glob and grep tools read (`yi_tools::ignore`, reached through `yi-tui`'s re-export), from the root and from each folder the walk enters, so a nested rule judges only what is under it and a `!` line puts back what an earlier line hid. The walk visits at most 20,000 entries, folders among them, so a checkout built of empty folders cannot walk past the cap either, and the popup's last row says how many files it kept, names the cap and says where the rest is, so the button costs a bounded read rather than a whole checkout. `⌥e` and the header button reach the same tree, and the tree is rooted where the focused pane works (`App::session_dir`, cwd then the session's root then the console's) rather than at the console's own launch root, so a lane worktree's session browses its own checkout and the file opens with that root as its workdir. A folder the walk reaches through a link is the folder it points at, with a canonical-path set keeping a link back up the tree from walking forever; a row is chosen by a press, so a pointer move neither moves the cursor nor repaints; a wheel notch scrolls the window and leaves the cursor where the user left it; PageUp/PageDown resolve a page in one pass over the drawn rows. `r` rereads the disk, and a root the walk cannot read says why instead of drawing an empty popup. A name that is not valid UTF-8 stays the path the walk found, so the row lists and folds and the editor refuses it by name rather than opening a shortened path that is not there. File size cost: `app.rs` and `render.rs` are past 1,000 lines at the fork, so the tree's keys, its clicks and the editor hand-off live in `app/files.rs` with the app-side helpers, `files.rs` keeps the model, the walk and the frame, both popups place themselves through one `palette::popup_in`, `render.rs` lost a pane's shadowing `body` binding to pay for the tree's arm, and `app.rs` pays for its `mod files;` line by folding a shadowing `let text` in its slash reply. The tree's keys, clicks and editor hand-off reach `overlay_press` for dismissal rather than repeating it. Rollback: revert this branch's feature commits (`de657cfda`, `aa09ebe34`, `427353039`, `d207b162f` and its answering commit); the tree's own code is `crates/console/src/files.rs`, `crates/console/src/app/files.rs` and the `Mode::Files` variant in `crates/console/src/model.rs` with its arms in `app.rs`, `app/mouse.rs` and `render.rs`; the only edits outside `crates/console` are three named re-exports (`yi-tools`' `ignore` module, `yi-runtime`'s and `yi-tui`'s `Ignore`) that nothing else reads. Pointing `Action::OpenEditor` back at `open_navigator("e ")` is not a rollback on its own: it restores the palette but leaves the walk, the key and mouse dispatch and an unconstructed `Mode::Files` compiled in as dead code, which `clippy -D warnings` fails under `just check`, and the chrome test asserts the mode. (Closes #1738) |
| 0.832.0 | 2026-10-31 | The permission modes now say what they do. Under `--yolo`, `write` and `edit` refused `/etc/nginx/nginx.conf` while `tee` wrote it, because the destroy list judged every write.
